\section{Macroscopic strength domain}\label{sec-strength-domain}

\subsection{Theoretical background}\label{theoretical-background}

For a porous medium with a solid phase obeying a \textbf{von Mises
criterion} with yield stress \(\sigma_0\), the local criterion reads
\(\sigma_d \leq \sigma_0\) where
\(\sigma_d = \sqrt{\uu{\sigma}':\uu{\sigma}'}\) is the local deviatoric
stress norm.

Using the second-order strain moments (see
Section~\ref{sec-second-moments}) and the Kreher--Suquet variational
result (\citeproc{ref-kreher1990}{Kreher, 1990};
\citeproc{ref-suquet1997}{Suquet, 1997}), the macroscopic strength
criterion can be expressed in closed form. For isotropic microstructures
(using the derivative w.r.t. \(\mu_s\) only), the macroscopic criterion
in terms of the mean stress
\(\Sigma_m = \tfrac{1}{3}\mathrm{tr}(\uu{\Sigma})\) and deviatoric
stress \(\Sigma_d = \sqrt{\uu{\Sigma}':\uu{\Sigma}'}\) is an ellipse:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-strength-ellipse}{
\frac{\Sigma_m^2}{a^2} + \frac{\Sigma_d^2}{b^2} \leq 1
}\end{equation}

whose semi-axes involve the derivatives of \(k^{hom}\) and \(\mu^{hom}\)
with respect to \(\mu_s\):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-strength-axes}{
a = \sigma_0\sqrt{\frac{f_s}{2\,A}},
\quad
b = \sigma_0\sqrt{\frac{f_s}{B}}
}\end{equation}

with

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-AB-def}{
A = \left(\frac{\mu_s}{k^{hom}}\right)^2\frac{\partial k^{hom}}{\partial \mu_s},
\quad
B = \left(\frac{\mu_s}{\mu^{hom}}\right)^2\frac{\partial \mu^{hom}}{\partial \mu_s}
}\end{equation}

where the normalized derivatives \(A\) and \(B\) measure the sensitivity
of the effective moduli to the solid shear stiffness.

\section{Failure criterion for porous
media}\label{failure-criterion-for-porous-media}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ ellipse\_radii(ks, mus, f, sch):}
    \CommentTok{"""Compute semi{-}axes of the macroscopic failure ellipse."""}
\NormalTok{    myrve }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    myrve[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(}\FloatTok{0.1}\NormalTok{), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                               prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: stiff\_kmu(ks, mus)\}, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f)}
\NormalTok{    myrve[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(}\FloatTok{0.1}\NormalTok{), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                              prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tZ4\}, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{f)}
\NormalTok{    khom, muhom }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{myrve, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{sch,}
\NormalTok{                             verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                             select\_best}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{).kmu}
\NormalTok{    dC }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize\_derivative(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{myrve, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{sch,}
\NormalTok{                               phase}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{, index}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, sym}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ISO)}
\NormalTok{    dCp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dC.paramsym(ISO)}
\NormalTok{    dkhomdmus }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dCp[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{*} \FloatTok{2.} \OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}
\NormalTok{    dmuhomdmus }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dCp[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{    A }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (mus }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ khom)}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dkhomdmus}
\NormalTok{    B }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (mus }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ muhom)}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dmuhomdmus}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ math.sqrt((}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{2.} \OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ A), math.sqrt((}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ B)}

\NormalTok{ks, mus }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.e6}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}
\NormalTok{ltheta }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, math.pi, }\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{f }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.15}

\NormalTok{fig, ax }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ sch }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [MT, DILD, SC, ASC, PCW, MAX]:}
\NormalTok{    a, b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipse\_radii(ks, mus, f, sch)}
\NormalTok{    ax.plot([a }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ math.cos(t) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ ltheta],}
\NormalTok{            [b }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ math.sin(t) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ ltheta],}
\NormalTok{            label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{sch}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, φ=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{f}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{ax.set\_aspect(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}equal\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.axhline(color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}k\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.axvline(color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}k\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$\textbackslash{}S}\VerbatimStringTok{igma\_m/}\DecValTok{\textbackslash{}s}\VerbatimStringTok{igma\_0}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$\textbackslash{}S}\VerbatimStringTok{igma\_d/}\DecValTok{\textbackslash{}s}\VerbatimStringTok{igma\_0}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.legend(loc}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}best\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{7_nonlinear/nonlinear_files/figure-pdf/cell-3-output-1.pdf}}

\section{Elastoplastic response of porous
media}\label{sec-elastoplastic}

\subsection{Modified secant method}\label{modified-secant-method}

The \textbf{modified secant method} (\citeproc{ref-suquet1997}{Suquet,
1997}) replaces each nonlinear phase by a linear elastic phase with a
\textbf{secant modulus} \(\mu^{sec}_r\) chosen so that the second-order
strain moment in the linearized composite matches the local yield
condition. For a von Mises matrix in phase \(r\) with yield stress
\(\sigma_0\):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-secant-modulus}{
\mu^{sec}_r = \begin{cases}
\mu_s & \text{if } \bar{\varepsilon}_r \leq \varepsilon_0 \\[4pt]
\dfrac{\sigma_0}{2\,\bar{\varepsilon}_r} & \text{otherwise}
\end{cases}
\quad\text{with}\quad \varepsilon_0 = \dfrac{\sigma_0}{2\mu_s}
}\end{equation}

where the equivalent strain \(\bar{\varepsilon}_r\) in layer \(r\) is
obtained from the second-order moment (see \ref{eq-kreher-aniso}):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-equivalent-strain}{
\bar{\varepsilon}_r^2 = \frac{1}{f_r}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial k^{hom}}{\partial \mu_r}\,E_v^2 + \frac{\partial \mu^{hom}}{\partial \mu_r}\,E_d^2\right)
}\end{equation}

with \(E_v = \mathrm{tr}(\uu{E})\) the volumetric macro-strain and
\(E_d^2 = \uu{E}':\uu{E}'\) the deviatoric macro-strain squared. The
nonlinear problem is solved by a fixed-point iteration: the matrix is
discretized into \(n\) concentric layers (via \texttt{sphere\_nlayers}),
each carrying a different secant modulus, and the self-consistent scheme
is applied until convergence.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ build\_rve\_ep(n, ks, mus, sigma0, f, tabed):}
\NormalTok{    eps0 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sigma0 }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{2.} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ mus)}
\NormalTok{    myrve }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tId4\})}
\NormalTok{    tabmu }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [mus }\ControlFlowTok{if} \BuiltInTok{abs}\NormalTok{(tabed[i]) }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=}\NormalTok{ eps0}
             \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ sigma0 }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{2.} \OperatorTok{*} \BuiltInTok{abs}\NormalTok{(tabed[i])) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(n)]}
\NormalTok{    myrve[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(}
\NormalTok{        radius}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        layer\_fractions}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[f] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [(}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ n }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ \_ }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(n)],}
\NormalTok{        prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: [tZ4] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [stiff\_kmu(ks, tabmu[i]) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(n)]\})}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ myrve}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ Sigma\_ep(vE, n, ks, mus, sigma0, f):}
\NormalTok{    tE }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ invKM(vE)}
\NormalTok{    Ev }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.trace(tE)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ Ev2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ Ev}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}
\NormalTok{    tEd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ tE }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (Ev }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.eye(}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ Ed2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(tEd }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tEd)}

    \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ buildsys(x):}
\NormalTok{        sys }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
\NormalTok{        myrve }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ build\_rve\_ep(n, ks, mus, sigma0, f, x)}
\NormalTok{        homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{myrve, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                   verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(n):}
\NormalTok{            dC }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize\_derivative(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{myrve, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                                        phase}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{, layer}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{i }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                                        index}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, sym}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ISO, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{            dCp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dC.paramsym(ISO)}
\NormalTok{            dkhomdmus }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dCp[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{*} \FloatTok{2.} \OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}
\NormalTok{            dmuhomdmus }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dCp[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{            fi }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ myrve[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{].layer\_fraction(i }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{            sys.append(math.sqrt((}\FloatTok{0.5} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dkhomdmus }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ Ev2}
                                  \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ dmuhomdmus }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ Ed2) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ fi) }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x[i])}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ sys}

\NormalTok{    x0 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{100.} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ sigma0 }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ mus }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.ones(n)).tolist()}
\NormalTok{    xsol }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fsolve(buildsys, x0)}
\NormalTok{    myrve }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ build\_rve\_ep(n, ks, mus, sigma0, f, xsol)}
\NormalTok{    Chom }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{myrve, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                      verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ Chom.array.dot(vE)}

\CommentTok{\# Triaxial loading: isotropic compression E = (Ev/3) * Id}
\NormalTok{tabE }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{4.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{vE0 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ KM(}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.eye(}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{ks, mus, sigma0, f }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{2.e3}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.e3}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.1}

\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{4.5}\NormalTok{))}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ n }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{    tabS }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ ee }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tabE:}
\NormalTok{        S }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ Sigma\_ep(ee }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ vE0, n, ks, mus, sigma0, f)}
\NormalTok{        tabS.append(np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(S[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{]) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.plot(tabE, tabS, label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"n=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{n}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{plt.xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{E\_v/3}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ plt.ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$\textbackslash{}S}\VerbatimStringTok{igma\_m}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ plt.legend()}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\part{𝗔𝗽𝗽𝗹𝗶𝗰𝗮𝘁𝗶𝗼𝗻𝘀}

\chapter{Transport properties}\label{sec-transport}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-important-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-important-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Objectives}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

This tutorial presents the homogenization of transport properties
(diffusivity, permeability, conductivity) using \emph{Echoes}. These are
described by 2nd-order tensors and can be homogenized with the same
framework as 4th-order elastic properties.

\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Imports}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ numpy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ np}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ echoes }\ImportTok{import} \OperatorTok{*}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ math}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\NormalTok{np.set\_printoptions(precision}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, suppress}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}

\section{Homogenization of 2nd-order tensor
properties}\label{homogenization-of-2nd-order-tensor-properties}

Transport properties (diffusivity \texttt{"D"},
permeability/conductivity \texttt{"K"}) are represented by 2nd-order
symmetric tensors. The \texttt{homogenize} function handles them via the
\texttt{prop} argument:

\begin{verbatim}
Dhom = homogenize(prop="D", rve=myrve, scheme=sch)
\end{verbatim}

The 2nd-order tensor properties are defined using \texttt{tensor}
objects with 3x3 matrices or the identity \texttt{tId2}.

\section{Diffusivity of porous media}\label{diffusivity-of-porous-media}

The effective diffusivity of a porous material with elongated pores can
be modeled as a function of porosity and pore aspect ratio.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Ds }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.1} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tId2   }\CommentTok{\# solid diffusivity}
\NormalTok{Dp }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.0} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tId2   }\CommentTok{\# pore diffusivity}

\NormalTok{myrve }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{myrve[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                           prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: Ds\})}
\NormalTok{myrve[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(}\FloatTok{0.1}\NormalTok{), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                          prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: Dp\})}

\NormalTok{lphi }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.6}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{51}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{fig, ax }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{))}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ omega }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.01}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.1}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{10.}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{    myrve[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{].shape }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ spheroidal(omega)}
\NormalTok{    lD }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ phi }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ lphi:}
\NormalTok{        myrve[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{].fraction }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ phi}
\NormalTok{        myrve[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{].fraction }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi}
        \ControlFlowTok{try}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{            D }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{myrve, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                           verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{            lD.append(}\BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(np.trace(D.array) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{))}
        \ControlFlowTok{except}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{            lD.append(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.plot(lphi, lD, label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"ω=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{omega}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}v}\VerbatimStringTok{arphi}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{D}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.legend()}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/transport_files/figure-pdf/cell-3-output-1.pdf}}

\section{Effect of the Interfacial Transition Zone
(ITZ)}\label{sec-transport-itz}

In mortar, the \textbf{Interfacial Transition Zone} (ITZ) is a thin
shell (\textasciitilde50 µm) of higher-porosity cement paste surrounding
each aggregate, with a locally higher diffusivity than the bulk paste.
Papadakis et al. (\citeproc{ref-papadakis1991}{1991}) noted that the
reduction in diffusivity due to impermeable aggregates can be offset ---
or even reversed --- by this more permeable surrounding shell.

\begin{figure}

\begin{minipage}[c]{0.55\linewidth}

\begin{figure}[H]

{\centering \pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/veritz.png}}

}

\subcaption{RVE of a mortar: cement paste matrix with aggregate
particles coated by ITZ shells.}

\end{figure}%

\end{minipage}%
%
\begin{minipage}[c]{0.45\linewidth}
The inclusion is modelled with \texttt{sphere\_nlayers} (see
Chapter~\ref{sec-nlayer_sphere}): an impermeable aggregate (layer 0,
radius \(R_{agg}\)) surrounded by an ITZ shell (layer 1, thickness
\(e_{ITZ}\)), both embedded in the cement paste matrix of reference
diffusivity \(D_{cp} = 1\).\end{minipage}%

\end{figure}%

Because the \texttt{sphere\_nlayers} inclusion covers aggregate + ITZ
shell, its effective volume fraction is larger than the bare aggregate
fraction \(f\):
\[f_{inc} = f\!\left(1 + \frac{e_{ITZ}}{R_{agg}}\right)^3\]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Ragg }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{5.e3}   \CommentTok{\# aggregate radius (µm)}
\NormalTok{eITZ }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{50.}    \CommentTok{\# ITZ thickness (µm)}

\CommentTok{\# RVE: cement paste matrix + (aggregate + ITZ) inclusion — MT scheme}
\NormalTok{ver\_itz }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tId2\}, matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"CEMENT"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ver\_itz[}\StringTok{"CEMENT"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tId2\})}
\NormalTok{ver\_itz[}\StringTok{"AGG"}\NormalTok{]    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(radii}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[Ragg, Ragg }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ eITZ],}
\NormalTok{                                   prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: [tZ2, tId2]\})}
\CommentTok{\# layer 0 = aggregate (D = 0), layer 1 = ITZ (D updated per run)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ Dhom\_itz(f, d\_itz):}
    \CommentTok{"""Normalised effective diffusivity Dhom/Dcp (MT scheme).}
\CommentTok{    f     : bare aggregate volume fraction}
\CommentTok{    d\_itz : Ditz/Dcp ratio}
\CommentTok{    """}
\NormalTok{    f2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ eITZ }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ Ragg)}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{3}        \CommentTok{\# effective inclusion fraction}
\NormalTok{    ver\_itz[}\StringTok{"CEMENT"}\NormalTok{].fraction }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f2}
\NormalTok{    ver\_itz[}\StringTok{"AGG"}\NormalTok{].fraction    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f2}
\NormalTok{    ver\_itz[}\StringTok{"AGG"}\NormalTok{].set\_prop(}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, d\_itz }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tId2)   }\CommentTok{\# update ITZ diffusivity}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ np.trace(homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_itz,}
\NormalTok{                               scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{).array) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{lf     }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.001}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.999}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{d\_list }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{100.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{80.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{60.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{50.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{40.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{20.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.001}\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{fig, ax }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{4.5}\NormalTok{))}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ d\_list:}
\NormalTok{    lbl }\OperatorTok{=} \VerbatimStringTok{fr"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{D\_}\CharTok{\{\{}\VerbatimStringTok{itz}\CharTok{\}\}}\VerbatimStringTok{/D\_}\CharTok{\{\{}\VerbatimStringTok{cp}\CharTok{\}\}}\VerbatimStringTok{=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{int}\NormalTok{(d) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ d }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=} \DecValTok{1} \ControlFlowTok{else} \DecValTok{0}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}
\NormalTok{    ax.plot(lf, [Dhom\_itz(f, d) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ f }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ lf], label}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{lbl)}

\CommentTok{\# Analytical references: purely impenetrable spheres (no ITZ)}
\NormalTok{ax.plot(lf, [(}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f)}\OperatorTok{**}\FloatTok{1.5}         \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ f }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ lf], }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}k{-}{-}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, label}\OperatorTok{=}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$}\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{1{-}f}\KeywordTok{)}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{3/2\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.plot(lf, [(}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f)}\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{.5}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{f)  }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ f }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ lf], }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}k:\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,  label}\OperatorTok{=}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$}\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{1{-}f}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{/}\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{1}\OperatorTok{+}\VerbatimStringTok{f/2}\KeywordTok{)}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"Aggregate fraction }\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{f}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{D}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}/D\_\{cp\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_xlim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.set\_ylim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{2.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.legend(fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, ncol}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/transport_files/figure-pdf/cell-5-output-1.pdf}}

The role of the aggregate+ITZ composite inclusion depends strongly on
\(D_{itz}/D_{cp}\):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{\(D_{itz}/D_{cp} \ll 1\)}: the ITZ is also impermeable;
  \(D^{hom}\) falls well below both bounds for simple impenetrable
  spheres.
\item
  \textbf{\(D_{itz}/D_{cp} \approx 50\)}: the two effects nearly cancel
  and \(D^{hom} \approx D_{cp}\) for moderate fractions --- consistent
  with the observation of Papadakis et al.
  (\citeproc{ref-papadakis1991}{1991}).
\item
  \textbf{\(D_{itz}/D_{cp} \gg 1\)}: the highly permeable ITZ network
  drives the effective diffusivity above the neat paste value.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{DUALDISC interface model}\label{dualdisc-interface-model}

A thin spherical shell of radius \(R\), thickness \(e \ll R\) and
diffusivity \(D_s\) can be replaced in the limit \(e \to 0\) by a
\textbf{zero-thickness interface} whose sole parameter is the
\textbf{transmissivity}:
\[\alpha = D_s \cdot e \qquad [\text{length}^2/\text{time} \times \text{length} = \text{length}^3/\text{time}]\]

The \texttt{DUALDISC} interface type implements this dual-discontinuity
condition: the normal flux is continuous across the interface, and the
concentration jump is proportional to \(1/\alpha\). This avoids the
explicit thin-layer and the associated volume-fraction correction
\(f\,(1+e_{ITZ}/R_{agg})^3\).

In this model the inclusion is simply the bare aggregate (1 layer,
\texttt{radii={[}Ragg{]}}); the ITZ is fully encoded in
\texttt{interf\_prop}:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# DUALDISC interface: transmissivity alpha = D\_ITZ * e\_ITZ  (Dcp = 1)}
\NormalTok{ver\_dd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tId2\}, matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"CEMENT"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ver\_dd[}\StringTok{"CEMENT"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tId2\})}
\NormalTok{ver\_dd[}\StringTok{"AGG"}\NormalTok{]    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(radii}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[Ragg], prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: [tZ2]\},}
\NormalTok{                                   interf\_prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: [[}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, DUALDISC]]\})}
\CommentTok{\# single interface at r=Ragg; alpha updated per run}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ Dhom\_dd(f, alpha):}
    \CommentTok{"""Normalised effective diffusivity via DUALDISC interface (MT scheme).}
\CommentTok{    f     : aggregate volume fraction}
\CommentTok{    alpha : transmissivity = D\_ITZ * e\_ITZ  (Dcp = 1)}
\CommentTok{    """}
\NormalTok{    ver\_dd[}\StringTok{"CEMENT"}\NormalTok{].fraction }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f}
\NormalTok{    ver\_dd[}\StringTok{"AGG"}\NormalTok{].fraction    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f}
\NormalTok{    ver\_dd[}\StringTok{"AGG"}\NormalTok{].set\_interf\_prop(}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, [alpha, DUALDISC])}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ np.trace(homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_dd, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{).array) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The two models agree closely for moderate aggregate fractions: the small
discrepancy at large \(f\) reflects the neglected volume
\(f\,[(1+e_{ITZ}/R_{agg})^3-1] \approx 3f\,e_{ITZ}/R_{agg}\) of the ITZ
shell (here \(\approx 3\%\)).

\section{Permeability of cracked
media}\label{permeability-of-cracked-media}

The permeability of cracked media is particularly important in
geomechanics. Cracks with high aperture-to-radius ratio create
preferential flow paths. This coupling between elasticity and transport
can be modeled by assigning both \texttt{"C"} and \texttt{"K"}
properties to the RVE phases (see Chapter~\ref{sec-cracked-media}).

\(\,\)

\chapter{Multiscale elasticity of a hydrating cement
paste}\label{sec-concrete}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-important-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-important-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Objectives}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

Following Sanahuja et al. (\citeproc{ref-sanahuja2007}{2007}), this
tutorial builds a \textbf{two-scale micromechanical model} of Portland
cement paste that predicts the evolution of the effective (drained and
undrained) Young's modulus from the water-to-cement ratio \(w/c\) and
the hydration degree \(\alpha\).

The key features of the model are:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A \textbf{self-consistent scheme} at each scale to handle the
  percolation of the outer C-S-H phase at setting.
\item
  A \textbf{generalized Mori-Tanaka scheme} (via
  \texttt{sphere\_nlayers}) at the paste scale to account for the
  inner/outer core-shell morphology.
\item
  A \textbf{poromechanics correction} (Biot theory) to convert drained
  into undrained moduli for comparison with ultra-sonic measurements.
\end{itemize}

\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Imports}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ numpy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ np}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ echoes }\ImportTok{import} \OperatorTok{*}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\NormalTok{np.set\_printoptions(precision}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, suppress}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Microstructural model}\label{sec-sanahuja-model}

Sanahuja et al. (\citeproc{ref-sanahuja2007}{2007}) distinguish two
C-S-H types at the paste scale:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Inner (high-density) hydrates} --- a porous polycrystal of
  oblate solid bricks (60×30×5 nm, aspect ratio
  \(\omega_i^s \approx 0.12\)) surrounding each anhydrous grain. Their
  porosity \(\varphi_i = 0.30\) is fixed throughout hydration.
\item
  \textbf{Outer (low-density) hydrates} --- oblate solid platelets
  (\(\omega_o^s = 0.033\), 4--8 bricks stacked) precipitating in the
  water-filled space. Their porosity \(\varphi_o\) evolves with
  hydration.
\end{itemize}

At the paste scale, composite inclusions (anhydrous core + inner layer)
are embedded in the outer matrix via a generalized Mori-Tanaka scheme
implemented with \texttt{sphere\_nlayers}.

\section{Powers hydration model and volume
fractions}\label{sec-sanahuja-vf}

The Powers model (\citeproc{ref-powers1946}{Powers and Brownyard, 1946})
provides \(f_a\), \(f_h\) and the total porosity \(f_p\):

\[
f_a = \frac{0.32(1-\alpha)}{w/c+0.32},\quad
f_h = \frac{0.68\,\alpha}{w/c+0.32},\quad
f_p = \frac{w/c-0.17\,\alpha}{w/c+0.32}
\]

Hydration stops when water (\(f_w = (w/c-0.4175\,\alpha)/(w/c+0.32)\))
or anhydrous is depleted: \(\alpha_{\max}=\min(1,\,w/c/0.4175)\).

The Tennis--Jennings model (\citeproc{ref-tennis2000}{Tennis and
Jennings, 2000}) gives the mass fraction \(m_{LD}\) of LD C-S-H: \[
m_{LD} = 3.017\,\alpha\,w/c - 1.347\,\alpha + 0.538
\]

Using \(\varphi_i = 0.30\) (fixed HD porosity), the five phase volumes
are:

\[
f_i^s = (1-m_{LD})(1-f_a-f_p),\quad
f_o^s = m_{LD}(1-f_a-f_p)
\] \[
f_i^p = \frac{\varphi_i}{1-\varphi_i}\,f_i^s,\quad
f_o^p = f_p - f_i^p
\] \[
f_i = f_i^s+f_i^p,\quad \varphi_o = \frac{f_o^p}{f_o^p+f_o^s}
\]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{rho\_a }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{3.13} \OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ kappa\_h }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{2.13} \OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ kappa\_w }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.31}
\NormalTok{phi\_i }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.30}   \CommentTok{\# inner (HD) fixed porosity}

\NormalTok{fa   }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, a: }\FloatTok{0.32} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{a) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (wc }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.32}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{fh   }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, a: }\FloatTok{0.68} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ a      }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (wc }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.32}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{fw   }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, a: (wc }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{0.4175}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{a) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (wc }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.32}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{fp   }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, a: (wc }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{0.17} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{a) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (wc }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.32}\NormalTok{)   }\CommentTok{\# total porosity}
\NormalTok{amax }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc:    }\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, wc }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{0.4175}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{mLD  }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, a: np.clip(}\FloatTok{3.017}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{a}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{wc }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{1.347}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{a }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.538}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ vf5(wc, alpha):}
    \CommentTok{"""Returns fis, fos, fip, fop, fi, phi\_o."""}
\NormalTok{    \_fa }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ \_fp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    fhs }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ \_fa }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ \_fp            }\CommentTok{\# total solid hydrates}
\NormalTok{    fis }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ mLD(wc, alpha)) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ fhs}
\NormalTok{    fos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mLD(wc, alpha)            }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ fhs}
\NormalTok{    fip }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ phi\_i }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi\_i) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ fis}
\NormalTok{    fop }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \_fp }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fip}
\NormalTok{    fi  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fis }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fip}
\NormalTok{    phi\_o }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fop }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (fop }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fos) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (fop }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fos) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}12} \ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ fis, fos, fip, fop, fi, phi\_o}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Scale 0: self-consistent estimates of inner and
outer}\label{sec-sc-inner-outer}

\subsection{Inner hydrates (fixed, SC)}\label{inner-hydrates-fixed-sc}

The inner porosity \(\varphi_i=0.30\) is independent of \(w/c\) and
\(\alpha\); \(C_\text{inner}\) is computed once:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{omega\_i }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.12}    \CommentTok{\# aspect ratio of HD solid bricks}
\NormalTok{omega\_o }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.033}   \CommentTok{\# aspect ratio of LD solid platelets}
\NormalTok{C\_sol   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{71.6}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.27}\NormalTok{)   }\CommentTok{\# solid C{-}S{-}H (bricks and platelets)}
\NormalTok{C\_anhyd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{135.}\NormalTok{,  }\FloatTok{0.3}\NormalTok{)   }\CommentTok{\# anhydrous clinker}

\NormalTok{ver\_in }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{ver\_in[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega\_i), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                             fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi\_i, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_sol\})}
\NormalTok{ver\_in[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{]  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{phi\_i, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tZ4\})}

\NormalTok{C\_inner }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_in, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                     verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Inner: E = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{C\_inner}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{E}\SpecialCharTok{:.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{ GPa,  k = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{C\_inner}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{k}\SpecialCharTok{:.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{ GPa,  "}
      \SpecialStringTok{f"mu = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{C\_inner}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{mu}\SpecialCharTok{:.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{ GPa"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
Inner: E = 31.00 GPa,  k = 19.93 GPa,  mu = 12.49 GPa
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{Outer hydrates (evolving \(\varphi_o\),
SC)}{Outer hydrates (evolving \textbackslash varphi\_o, SC)}}\label{outer-hydrates-evolving-varphi_o-sc}

The outer porosity \(\varphi_o(w/c,\alpha)\) varies with hydration.
Setting occurs when \(\varphi_o\) reaches the SC percolation threshold
(controlled by \(\omega_o^s\)):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ver\_out }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{ver\_out[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega\_o), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                              prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_sol\})}
\NormalTok{ver\_out[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{]  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tZ4\})}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ C\_outer(phi\_o):}
\NormalTok{    ver\_out[}\StringTok{"SOLID"}\NormalTok{].fraction }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi\_o}
\NormalTok{    ver\_out[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{].fraction  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ phi\_o}
\NormalTok{    C }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_out, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                   verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ np.isnan(C.k): }\ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ tZ4}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ C}

\CommentTok{\# Illustration: outer stiffness vs porosity}
\NormalTok{lphi }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{60}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{lE\_in  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [C\_inner.E] }\OperatorTok{*} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(lphi)}
\NormalTok{lE\_out }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ phi }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ lphi:}
\NormalTok{    Co }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C\_outer(phi)}
\NormalTok{    lE\_out.append(Co.E }\ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ np.isnan(Co.E) }\ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{fig, ax }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{4.5}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{ax.plot(lphi, lE\_out, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}b{-}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,  label}\OperatorTok{=}\VerbatimStringTok{r"outer }\KeywordTok{(}\DecValTok{$}\ErrorTok{\textbackslash{}}\VerbatimStringTok{omega\_o}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{s=0}\DecValTok{.}\VerbatimStringTok{033}\DecValTok{$}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.axhline(C\_inner.E, color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}r\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, ls}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}{-}{-}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, label}\OperatorTok{=}\VerbatimStringTok{r"inner }\KeywordTok{(}\DecValTok{$}\ErrorTok{\textbackslash{}}\VerbatimStringTok{omega\_i}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{s=0}\DecValTok{.}\VerbatimStringTok{12}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{, fixed}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"Porosity }\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}v}\VerbatimStringTok{arphi}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{E}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{GPa}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_title(}\StringTok{"SC estimates of inner and outer C{-}S{-}H"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.legend()}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/concrete_files/figure-pdf/cell-5-output-1.pdf}}

\section{Scale 1: cement paste (generalized
MT)}\label{sec-sanahuja-paste}

At the paste scale, composite spheres (anhydrous core of radius \(R_a\)
coated by an inner layer up to radius \(R_\text{ref}=1\)) are embedded
in the outer matrix via the Mori-Tanaka scheme. The radius ratio is
fixed by the volume fraction constraint:

\[
\frac{R_a}{R_\text{ref}} = \left(\frac{f_a}{f_a+f_i}\right)^{1/3}
\]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ C\_paste(wc, alpha}\OperatorTok{={-}}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{):}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{: alpha }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ amax(wc)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}
\NormalTok{    \_fa }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    fis, fos, fip, fop, fi, phi\_o }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ vf5(wc, alpha)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ fop }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ phi\_o }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=} \FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}

\NormalTok{    Cout }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C\_outer(phi\_o)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ Cout }\KeywordTok{is}\NormalTok{ tZ4 }\KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ np.isnan(Cout.k): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}

\NormalTok{    f\_outer }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ \_fa }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fi       }\CommentTok{\# outer fraction in paste}
\NormalTok{    f\_inc   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \_fa }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fi             }\CommentTok{\# sphere\_nlayers fraction}
\NormalTok{    Ra      }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ((\_fa }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ f\_inc)}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ f\_inc }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}12} \ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{    ver }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"OUTER"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"OUTER"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{f\_outer,}
\NormalTok{                             prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: Cout\})}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"INC"}\NormalTok{]   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(radii}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[Ra, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{],}
\NormalTok{                                  fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{f\_inc,}
\NormalTok{                                  prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: [C\_anhyd, C\_inner]\})}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{wc\_list }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.25}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.35}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.45}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.55}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{fig, ax }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{4.5}\NormalTok{))}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ wc }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ wc\_list:}
\NormalTok{    la }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.999} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ amax(wc), }\DecValTok{80}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    lE }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ alpha }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ la:}
\NormalTok{        C }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C\_paste(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        lE.append(C.E }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ C }\KeywordTok{is} \KeywordTok{not} \VariableTok{None} \KeywordTok{and} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ np.isnan(C.E) }\ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.plot(la, lE, label}\OperatorTok{=}\VerbatimStringTok{fr"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{w/c=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{wc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}a}\VerbatimStringTok{lpha}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{E}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{GPa}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_title(}\StringTok{"Drained Young\textquotesingle{}s modulus of cement paste"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.set\_xlim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.set\_ylim(bottom}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.legend()}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/concrete_files/figure-pdf/cell-7-output-1.pdf}}

The model correctly predicts:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a \textbf{setting threshold} (onset of stiffness) at
  \(\alpha \approx 0.1\)--\(0.25\) depending on \(w/c\), controlled by
  the SC percolation of the outer phase;
\item
  a monotonic increase of \(E^{hom}\) up to \(\alpha_{\max}\);
\item
  lower asymptotic stiffness for higher \(w/c\) (more residual porosity
  at full hydration).
\end{itemize}

\section{Undrained moduli}\label{sec-sanahuja-undrained}

Ultra-sonic measurements probe the \textbf{undrained} elastic moduli of
the saturated paste. The conversion uses Biot poromechanics (see
Section~\ref{sec-concentration-keyword}). For a porous medium with
\textbf{homogeneous isotropic solid} (moduli \(k_s\), \(\mu_s\)):

\[
b = 1 - \frac{k^{hom}}{k_s}, \qquad
M = \frac{k_s}{b - \varphi}, \qquad
k^u = k^{hom} + M b^2, \qquad
\mu^u = \mu^{hom}
\]

Applied scale by scale (inner, then outer with
\(k_s = k_\text{solid CSH}\); paste with the undrained inner/outer as
inputs):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ks\_sol, mus\_sol }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C\_sol.kmu   }\CommentTok{\# solid C{-}S{-}H}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ undrained\_kmu(k\_hom, mu\_hom, k\_s, phi):}
    \CommentTok{"""Biot undrained bulk modulus."""}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ k\_hom }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}12} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ phi }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{:}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ k\_hom, mu\_hom}
\NormalTok{    b }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ k\_hom }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ k\_s}
\NormalTok{    M }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ k\_s }\OperatorTok{/} \BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(b }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi, }\FloatTok{1.e{-}12}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ k\_hom }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ M }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ b}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, mu\_hom}

\CommentTok{\# Undrained inner (fixed)}
\NormalTok{k\_iu, mu\_iu }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ undrained\_kmu(C\_inner.k, C\_inner.mu, ks\_sol, phi\_i)}
\NormalTok{C\_inner\_u }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_kmu(k\_iu, mu\_iu)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ C\_paste\_undrained(wc, alpha}\OperatorTok{={-}}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{):}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{: alpha }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ amax(wc)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}
\NormalTok{    \_fa }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    fis, fos, fip, fop, fi, phi\_o }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ vf5(wc, alpha)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ fop }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ phi\_o }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=} \FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}

\NormalTok{    Cout }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C\_outer(phi\_o)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ Cout }\KeywordTok{is}\NormalTok{ tZ4 }\KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ np.isnan(Cout.k): }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}

    \CommentTok{\# Undrained outer}
\NormalTok{    k\_ou, mu\_ou }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ undrained\_kmu(Cout.k, Cout.mu, ks\_sol, phi\_o)}
\NormalTok{    Cout\_u }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_kmu(k\_ou, mu\_ou)}

\NormalTok{    f\_outer }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ \_fa }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fi}
\NormalTok{    f\_inc   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \_fa }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fi}
\NormalTok{    Ra      }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (\_fa }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ f\_inc)}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ f\_inc }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}12} \ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}

\NormalTok{    ver }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"OUTER"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"OUTER"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{f\_outer,}
\NormalTok{                             prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: Cout\_u\})}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"INC"}\NormalTok{]   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(radii}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[Ra, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{],}
\NormalTok{                                  fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{f\_inc,}
\NormalTok{                                  prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: [C\_anhyd, C\_inner\_u]\})}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{fig, axes }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{))}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ wc }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ wc\_list:}
\NormalTok{    la }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.999} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ amax(wc), }\DecValTok{80}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    lEd, lEu }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [], []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ alpha }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ la:}
\NormalTok{        Cd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C\_paste(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        Cu }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C\_paste\_undrained(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        lEd.append(Cd.E }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ Cd }\KeywordTok{is} \KeywordTok{not} \VariableTok{None} \KeywordTok{and} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ np.isnan(Cd.E) }\ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        lEu.append(Cu.E }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ Cu }\KeywordTok{is} \KeywordTok{not} \VariableTok{None} \KeywordTok{and} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ np.isnan(Cu.E) }\ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    axes[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].plot(la, lEd, label}\OperatorTok{=}\VerbatimStringTok{fr"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{w/c=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{wc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    axes[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].plot(la, lEu, label}\OperatorTok{=}\VerbatimStringTok{fr"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{w/c=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{wc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ ax, title }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{zip}\NormalTok{(axes, [}\StringTok{"Drained $E\^{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{hom\}}\StringTok{$"}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{"Undrained $E\^{}\{hom,u\}$"}\NormalTok{]):}
\NormalTok{    ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}a}\VerbatimStringTok{lpha}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{E}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{GPa}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.set\_title(title)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.set\_xlim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.set\_ylim(bottom}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax.legend()}

\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/concrete_files/figure-pdf/cell-9-output-1.pdf}}

The undrained moduli are systematically higher than the drained ones,
the difference being largest at low hydration degrees where the pore
network is still well connected. At full hydration the difference
vanishes as \(\varphi \to 0\) and \(b \to 0\).

\(\,\)

\chapter{Cement paste: chloride diffusivity and
elasticity}\label{sec-cement-paste-diffusion}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-important-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-important-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Objectives}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

Following Achour et al. (\citeproc{ref-achourCBM2020}{2020}), this
tutorial builds a multi-scale micromechanical model of Portland cement
paste that \textbf{simultaneously predicts its elastic moduli and
chloride diffusivity} from a single microstructural description as a
function of the water-to-cement ratio \(w/c\) and the hydration degree
\(\alpha\).

Two models of increasing complexity are presented:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  an \textbf{engineering model} (two scales): hydrate foam + clinker;
\item
  a \textbf{detailed model} (three scales): C-S-H gel → hydrate layers →
  cement paste.
\end{itemize}

The self-consistent scheme is used at each scale to capture the
\textbf{percolation} of both the solid skeleton (governing stiffness)
and the pore network (governing diffusivity) at realistic hydration
degrees.

\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Imports}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ numpy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ np}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ echoes }\ImportTok{import} \OperatorTok{*}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\NormalTok{np.set\_printoptions(precision}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, suppress}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Volume fractions: Powers' hydration model}\label{sec-powers-vf}

The volume fractions of the main phases are derived from Powers'
hydration model as functions of \(w/c\) and \(\alpha\)
(\citeproc{ref-achourCBM2020}{Achour et al., 2020}):

\[
f_a = \frac{1-\alpha}{1+\rho_a\,w/c}, \quad
f_h = \frac{\kappa_h\,\alpha}{1+\rho_a\,w/c}, \quad
f_{cp} = 1 - f_a - f_h
\]

with \(\rho_a=3.13\) (clinker density), \(\kappa_h=2.13\) (hydrate
expansion factor). When excess water is available, hydration stops when
the pore space is filled:

\[
\alpha_{\max} = \min\!\left(1,\;\frac{\rho_a\,w/c}{\kappa_h-1}\right)
\]

The Tennis--Jennings correlation (\citeproc{ref-tennis2000}{Tennis and
Jennings, 2000}) provides the mass ratio \(M_r\) of low-density (LD) to
total C-S-H:

\[
M_r = \min\!\Bigl(1,\;0.538 + \alpha(3.017\,w/c - 1.347)\Bigr)
\]

This splits the hydration products into an \textbf{inner layer}
(HD-C-S-H, \(\phi_{HD}=0.24\)) and an \textbf{outer layer} (LD-C-S-H,
\(\phi_{LD}=0.37\)), with a fraction \(\eta=20\%\) of crystalline
hydrates (portlandite) in each.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Powers\textquotesingle{} hydration model constants}
\NormalTok{rho\_a   }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{3.13}   \CommentTok{\# clinker{-}to{-}water density ratio}
\NormalTok{kappa\_h }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{2.13}   \CommentTok{\# volume of hydrates / unit volume of clinker}
\NormalTok{eta     }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.20}   \CommentTok{\# crystalline hydrate fraction in hydration products}
\NormalTok{phi\_HD  }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.24}   \CommentTok{\# HD{-}CSH gel porosity}
\NormalTok{phi\_LD  }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.37}   \CommentTok{\# LD{-}CSH gel porosity}
\NormalTok{phi\_od0 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.63}   \CommentTok{\# initial SCP fraction in outer domain (Ma 2014 fit)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ alphamax(wc):}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, wc }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rho\_a }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (kappa\_h }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{))}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha):   }\ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ alpha) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ rho\_a }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ wc)}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fh(wc, alpha):   }\ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ kappa\_h }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ rho\_a }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ wc)}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fcp(wc, alpha):  }\ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha) }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fh(wc, alpha))}

\CommentTok{\# Tennis{-}Jennings LD/HD mass ratio and LD volume fraction}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ mLD(wc, alpha):}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.538} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{3.017} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ wc }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{1.347}\NormalTok{))}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ muLD(wc, alpha):}
\NormalTok{    mr }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mLD(wc, alpha)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ mr }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi\_HD) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi\_LD }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ mr }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (phi\_LD }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi\_HD))}

\CommentTok{\# Inner (HD{-}based) and outer (LD{-}based) hydration products}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fihp(wc, alpha): }\ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ muLD(wc, alpha)) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ fh(wc, alpha)}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fohp(wc, alpha): }\ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ muLD(wc, alpha) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ fh(wc, alpha)}

\CommentTok{\# SCP (small, 3{-}100 nm) and LCP (large, \textgreater{}100 nm) capillary pore fractions}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ psif(wc, alpha):}
\NormalTok{    denom }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha) }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fihp(wc, alpha)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \FloatTok{0.} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ denom }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}12} \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ fohp(wc, alpha) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ denom}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fscp(wc, alpha):}
\NormalTok{    phi }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ phi\_od0 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ psif(wc, alpha))}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ fohp(wc, alpha) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ phi }\OperatorTok{/} \BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi, }\FloatTok{1.e{-}12}\NormalTok{)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ flcp(wc, alpha):}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, fcp(wc, alpha) }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fscp(wc, alpha))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Engineering model}\label{sec-cement-eng-model}

The engineering model (\citeproc{ref-achourCBM2020}{Achour et al.,
2020}; \citeproc{ref-pichler2011}{Pichler and Hellmich, 2011}) involves
two homogenization steps:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Level I --- hydrate foam} (self-consistent): an aging
  disordered assemblage of hydration products and capillary pores. The
  hydrates are modeled as oblate spheroids (\(\omega_h = 0.013\)) so
  that the solid phase percolates at the experimentally observed setting
  degree; the capillary pores are prolate (\(\omega_{cp} = 6\)) to
  maintain a connected pore network throughout hydration.
\item
  \textbf{Level II --- cement paste} (Mori-Tanaka): spherical anhydrous
  clinker inclusions embedded in the hydrate foam matrix.
\end{enumerate}

The same phase properties serve both the mechanical and the diffusion
problems. All diffusivities are normalized by the bulk-water value
\(D_\text{bulk}=1\).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Material properties — engineering model}
\NormalTok{C\_anhyd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{135.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.3}\NormalTok{)    }\CommentTok{\# anhydrous clinker}
\NormalTok{C\_hyd   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{25.3}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.29}\NormalTok{)   }\CommentTok{\# hydration products (calibrated, see text)}
\NormalTok{D\_hyd   }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{5.04e{-}4} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tId2          }\CommentTok{\# hydrate diffusivity (calibrated to Yu 1991)}
\NormalTok{D\_cap   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ tId2                    }\CommentTok{\# capillary pore: D\_bulk = 1}

\CommentTok{\# Aspect ratios (calibrated from setting experiments)}
\NormalTok{omega\_h  }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.013}   \CommentTok{\# oblate hydrates}
\NormalTok{omega\_cp }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{6.}      \CommentTok{\# prolate capillary pores}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ engineering\_model(wc, alpha}\OperatorTok{={-}}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{):}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{: alpha }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ alphamax(wc)}
\NormalTok{    \_fa  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    \_fh  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fh(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    \_fcp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fcp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    ft   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \_fh }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ \_fcp            }\CommentTok{\# foam total fraction (= 1 {-} fa)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ ft }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{: }\ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{, }\VariableTok{None}

    \CommentTok{\# Level I: hydrate foam (SC)}
\NormalTok{    ver\_foam }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{    ver\_foam[}\StringTok{"HYD"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega\_h),  symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                                fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\_fh  }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ ft, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_hyd, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_hyd\})}
\NormalTok{    ver\_foam[}\StringTok{"CAP"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega\_cp), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                                fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\_fcp }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ ft, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tZ4,   }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_cap\})}
    \ControlFlowTok{try}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        C\_foam }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_foam, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                            verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        D\_foam }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_foam, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                            verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
        \CommentTok{\# Skip if foam has not yet percolated (zero stiffness → singular MT matrix)}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ C\_foam.k }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{:}
            \ControlFlowTok{return} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, np.trace(D\_foam.array) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}

        \CommentTok{\# Level II: cement paste (MT — clinker inclusions in foam matrix)}
\NormalTok{        ver\_paste }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"FOAM"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        ver\_paste[}\StringTok{"FOAM"}\NormalTok{]   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ \_fa,}
\NormalTok{                                        prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_foam, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_foam\})}
\NormalTok{        ver\_paste[}\StringTok{"CLINKER"}\NormalTok{]}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\_fa,}
\NormalTok{                                        prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_anhyd, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tZ2\})}
\NormalTok{        C\_cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_paste, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        D\_cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_paste, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ C\_cp.E, np.trace(D\_cp.array) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}
    \ControlFlowTok{except} \PreprocessorTok{Exception}\NormalTok{:}
        \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{, }\VariableTok{None}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{wc\_list }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.30}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.40}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.50}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.60}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{n\_alpha }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{40}

\NormalTok{fig, (ax1, ax2) }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{))}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ wc }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ wc\_list:}
\NormalTok{    la }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.02}\NormalTok{, alphamax(wc), n\_alpha)}
\NormalTok{    lE, lD }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [], []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ alpha }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ la:}
\NormalTok{        E, D }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ engineering\_model(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        lE.append(E }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ E }\KeywordTok{is} \KeywordTok{not} \VariableTok{None} \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ np.nan)}
\NormalTok{        lD.append(D }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ D }\KeywordTok{is} \KeywordTok{not} \VariableTok{None} \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ np.nan)}
\NormalTok{    ax1.plot(la, lE,   label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"w/c=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{wc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax2.semilogy(la, lD, label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"w/c=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{wc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{ax1.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}a}\VerbatimStringTok{lpha}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax1.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{E}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{GPa}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax1.set\_xlim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax1.set\_ylim(bottom}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax1.set\_title(}\StringTok{"Young\textquotesingle{}s modulus"}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax1.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax1.legend()}

\NormalTok{ax2.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}a}\VerbatimStringTok{lpha}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax2.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{D}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}/D\_\{bulk\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax2.set\_xlim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax2.set\_title(}\StringTok{"Chloride diffusivity"}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax2.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, which}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"both"}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ax2.legend()}

\NormalTok{plt.suptitle(}\StringTok{"Engineering model"}\NormalTok{, y}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.01}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
