\section{Detailed model}\label{sec-cement-detailed-model}

The detailed three-scale model explicitly tracks C-S-H gel
microstructure at the nanometre scale, the inner/outer hydrate layers at
the micrometre scale, and the cement paste assembly at the grain scale.

\subsection{Level 0: C-S-H gels}\label{sec-csh-gel}

Both HD- and LD-C-S-H gels are modeled as a SC assemblage of:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Oblate solid bricks} (\(\omega_s = 0.12\), \(E=63\) GPa,
  \(\nu=0.27\), \(D=0\)) --- based on the 60×30×5 nm dimensions observed
  for elementary C-S-H particles.
\item
  \textbf{Prolate gel pores} (\(\omega_p = 1/\omega_s \approx 8.3\),
  \(D_{gel} = 0.025\,D_{bulk}\)) --- the elongated shape is required to
  ensure non-zero diffusivity through both gel types (oblate or
  spherical pores would yield zero diffusivity at the HD-CSH porosity
  \(\phi_{HD}=0.24\)).
\end{itemize}

The gel properties are fixed (independent of \(w/c\) and \(\alpha\)) and
pre-computed once.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Level 0: C{-}S{-}H gel microstructure}
\NormalTok{omega\_s }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.12}          \CommentTok{\# solid C{-}S{-}H brick (oblate)}
\NormalTok{omega\_p }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ omega\_s  }\CommentTok{\# gel pore (prolate, ≈ 8.3)}
\NormalTok{C\_brick  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{63.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.27}\NormalTok{)    }\CommentTok{\# solid C{-}S{-}H brick}
\NormalTok{C\_crystal}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{42.3}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.324}\NormalTok{)  }\CommentTok{\# crystalline hydrate (portlandite)}
\NormalTok{D\_gel    }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.025} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tId2            }\CommentTok{\# gel pore diffusivity}
\NormalTok{D\_cap    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ tId2                    }\CommentTok{\# capillary pore diffusivity = D\_bulk}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ homogenize\_csh(phi):}
    \CommentTok{"""SC estimate of a C{-}S{-}H gel foam at gel porosity phi."""}
\NormalTok{    ver }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"BRICK"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega\_s), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                             fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_brick, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tZ2\})}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{]  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega\_p), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                             fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{phi,     prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tZ4,    }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_gel\})}
\NormalTok{    C\_g }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                     epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    D\_g }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                     epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ C\_g, D\_g}

\CommentTok{\# Pre{-}compute HD and LD C{-}S{-}H gel properties (fixed)}
\NormalTok{C\_HD, D\_HD }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize\_csh(phi\_HD)}
\NormalTok{C\_LD, D\_LD }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize\_csh(phi\_LD)}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"HD{-}CSH: E = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{C\_HD}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{E}\SpecialCharTok{:.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{ GPa,  D/D\_bulk = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{np}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{trace(D\_HD.array)}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\SpecialCharTok{:.4e\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"LD{-}CSH: E = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{C\_LD}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{E}\SpecialCharTok{:.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{ GPa,  D/D\_bulk = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{np}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{trace(D\_LD.array)}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\SpecialCharTok{:.4e\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
HD-CSH: E = 31.04 GPa,  D/D_bulk = 4.4318e-04
LD-CSH: E = 17.59 GPa,  D/D_bulk = 1.5964e-03
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Level I: inner and outer hydrate
layers}\label{sec-hydrate-layers}

\textbf{Inner layer} (HD-C-S-H + nano-crystals, SC, spherical
particles):

\[
\text{inner} = \text{SC}\!\left[(1-\eta)\,\text{HD-CSH}_\text{foam} +
\eta\,\text{nano-crystals}\right]
\]

\textbf{Outer C-S-H layer} (LD-C-S-H + small capillary pores, SC):

\[
\text{outer-CSH} = \text{SC}\!\left[\frac{(1-\eta)f_{ohp}}{f_{scp}+(1-\eta)f_{ohp}}\,
\text{LD-CSH}_{\omega_{LD}=0.14} + \frac{f_{scp}}{f_{scp}+(1-\eta)f_{ohp}}\,
\text{SCP}_\text{spherical}\right]
\]

The oblate LD-C-S-H foam aspect ratio \(\omega_{LD} = 0.14\) controls
the setting threshold of the outer layer. Micro-crystals (fraction
\(\eta f_{ohp}/(f_{scp}+f_{ohp})\) of the outer domain) are folded into
the outer layer by a second SC step.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{omega\_LD }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.14}    \CommentTok{\# oblate LD{-}CSH foam (calibrated to setting data)}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ inner\_layer\_props(wc, alpha):}
    \CommentTok{"""SC: HD{-}CSH gel + spherical nano{-}crystals."""}
\NormalTok{    eta\_inner }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ eta   }\CommentTok{\# crystal fraction in inner layer}
\NormalTok{    ver }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"CSH"}\NormalTok{]     }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ eta\_inner,}
\NormalTok{                               prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_HD, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_HD\})}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"CRYSTAL"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{eta\_inner,}
\NormalTok{                               prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_crystal, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tZ2\})}
\NormalTok{    C\_in }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                      epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    D\_in }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                      epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ C\_in, D\_in}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ outer\_layer\_props(wc, alpha):}
    \CommentTok{"""SC: LD{-}CSH gel + SCP → outer CSH; then SC: outer CSH + micro{-}crystals."""}
\NormalTok{    \_fohp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fohp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    \_fscp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fscp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    f\_ldcsh }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ eta) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ \_fohp        }\CommentTok{\# LD{-}CSH foam volume in outer domain}
\NormalTok{    f\_outer\_total }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f\_ldcsh }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ \_fscp     }\CommentTok{\# LD{-}CSH + SCP}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ f\_outer\_total }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{1.e{-}12}\NormalTok{:}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ C\_LD, D\_LD}

\NormalTok{    phi\_scp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \_fscp }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ f\_outer\_total     }\CommentTok{\# SCP fraction within LD{-}CSH+SCP mix}

    \CommentTok{\# Step 1: SC of oblate LD{-}CSH foam + spherical SCPs}
\NormalTok{    ver\_csh\_scp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{    ver\_csh\_scp[}\StringTok{"LDCSH"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega\_LD), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                                     fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ phi\_scp,}
\NormalTok{                                     prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_LD, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_LD\})}
\NormalTok{    ver\_csh\_scp[}\StringTok{"SCP"}\NormalTok{]   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{phi\_scp,}
\NormalTok{                                     prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tZ4,  }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_cap\})}
\NormalTok{    C\_csh\_scp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_csh\_scp, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                            verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    D\_csh\_scp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_csh\_scp, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                            verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}

    \CommentTok{\# Step 2: SC of outer CSH layer + spherical micro{-}crystals}
\NormalTok{    eta\_outer }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ eta }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ \_fohp }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (\_fohp }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ \_fscp)  }\CommentTok{\# micro{-}crystal fraction}
\NormalTok{    ver\_outer }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{    ver\_outer[}\StringTok{"CSH"}\NormalTok{]     }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ eta\_outer,}
\NormalTok{                                     prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_csh\_scp, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_csh\_scp\})}
\NormalTok{    ver\_outer[}\StringTok{"CRYSTAL"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{eta\_outer,}
\NormalTok{                                     prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_crystal, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: tZ2\})}
\NormalTok{    C\_out }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_outer, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                        verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    D\_out }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_outer, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                        verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ C\_out, D\_out}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Level II: cement paste}\label{sec-paste-level-ii}

At the largest scale, the cement paste is assembled using a generalized
self-consistent scheme with two inclusion types:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a \textbf{\texttt{sphere\_nlayers}} composite: anhydrous clinker
  (core) + inner layer + outer layer, with layer thickness ratios
  determined by the volume fractions;
\item
  spherical \textbf{large capillary pores} (LCPs, \(D = D_{bulk}\)).
\end{itemize}

The \texttt{sphere\_nlayers} constructor with \texttt{layer\_fractions}
takes the three layer volume fractions (relative to the whole paste) and
automatically computes the layer radii.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ detailed\_model(wc, alpha}\OperatorTok{={-}}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{):}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{: alpha }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ alphamax(wc)}
    \ControlFlowTok{try}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        \_fa   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fa(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        \_fihp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fihp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        \_fohp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fohp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        \_fscp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fscp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        \_flcp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ flcp(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        f\_layers }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \_fa }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ \_fihp }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ \_fohp }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ \_fscp  }\CommentTok{\# total sphere\_nlayers fraction}

        \CommentTok{\# Level I properties}
\NormalTok{        C\_inner, D\_inner }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ inner\_layer\_props(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{        C\_outer, D\_outer }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ outer\_layer\_props(wc, alpha)}

        \CommentTok{\# Level II: generalized SC — composite sphere + LCPs}
\NormalTok{        ver\_paste }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{        ver\_paste[}\StringTok{"LAYERS"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(}
\NormalTok{            radius}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{            layer\_fractions}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[\_fa, \_fihp, \_fohp }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ \_fscp],}
\NormalTok{            fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{f\_layers,}
\NormalTok{            prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: [C\_anhyd, C\_inner, C\_outer],}
                  \StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: [tZ2,    D\_inner, D\_outer]\})}
\NormalTok{        ver\_paste[}\StringTok{"LCP"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\_flcp,}
\NormalTok{                                     prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tZ4, }\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{: D\_cap\})}

\NormalTok{        C\_cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_paste, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                          verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        D\_cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"D"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver\_paste, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                          verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ C\_cp.E, np.trace(D\_cp.array) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}
    \ControlFlowTok{except} \PreprocessorTok{Exception}\NormalTok{:}
        \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{, }\VariableTok{None}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Comparison of both models}\label{sec-model-comparison}

The two models predict consistent trends for both elastic moduli and
chloride diffusivity throughout hydration. The engineering model is
significantly faster but relies on effective hydrate properties
calibrated from mature paste data. The detailed model is richer in
microstructural content and yields closer agreement with experimental
data across the full hydration range and a wider range of \(w/c\)
(\citeproc{ref-achourCBM2020}{Achour et al., 2020}).

For a given \(w/c\), both models predict:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a \textbf{percolation threshold} in stiffness around the
  experimentally observed setting degree (controlled by the calibrated
  oblate aspect ratios);
\item
  a \textbf{sharp drop} in diffusivity at intermediate to high hydration
  degrees, as the large capillary pore network becomes disconnected and
  diffusion is forced through the poorly-diffusive gel pores.
\end{itemize}

\section{SC percolation diagrams: role of inclusion
shapes}\label{sec-percolation-diagrams}

In the SC scheme for a two-phase assemblage of \textbf{solid spheroids}
(aspect ratio \(\omega_s\)) and \textbf{pore spheroids} (aspect ratio
\(\omega_p\)), the elastic and diffusion percolation thresholds depend
strongly on the shape of both phases. These diagrams --- established in
Achour et al. (\citeproc{ref-achourCBM2020}{2020}) --- provide the
geometrical basis for calibrating the aspect ratios \(\omega_h\) and
\(\omega_{cp}\) of the engineering model.

Two percolation thresholds are computed for a normalised medium (solid:
unit Young's modulus, Poisson's ratio \(\nu\); pores:
\(\uuuu{C}_p=\uuuu{0}\), \(\uu{D}_p=\uu{I}\)):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Elastic threshold} \(\phi_e^{\rm elas}\): the critical pore
  fraction above which \(\uuuu{C}^{\rm hom}=\uuuu{0}\) (solid skeleton
  ceases to carry load). Setting
  \(\uuuu{T}_p(\nu)=\uuuu{C}(\nu):(\uuuu{I}-\uuuu{S}_p(\nu))^{-1}\), the
  SC equation at \(\uuuu{C}^{\rm hom}\to\uuuu{0}^+\) reduces to the
  coupled system in \((f_s,\nu)\):
\end{itemize}

\[
f_s = \frac{\tr(\uuuu{J}:\uuuu{T}_p)}{\tr(\uuuu{J}:\uuuu{P}_s^{-1})+\tr(\uuuu{J}:\uuuu{T}_p)},
\qquad
f_s\,\tr(\uuuu{K}:\uuuu{P}_s^{-1}) = (1-f_s)\,\tr(\uuuu{K}:\uuuu{T}_p)
\]

where \(\uuuu{P}_s=\uuuu{P}(\omega_s,\uuuu{C}(\nu))\) is the Hill
tensor; \(\phi_e^{\rm elas}=1-f_s\).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Diffusion threshold} \(\phi_e^{\rm diff}\): the critical
  porosity above which the pore network is connected
  (\(\uu{D}^{\rm hom}>\uu{0}\)). It follows analytically from the
  second-order SC equation at \(\uu{D}^{\rm hom}\to\uu{0}^+\):
\end{itemize}

\[
\phi_e^{\rm diff} = \frac{\tr(\uu{I}-\uu{S}_s)^{-1}}{\tr\,\uu{P}_p^{-1}+\tr(\uu{I}-\uu{S}_s)^{-1}}
\]

where \(\uu{P}_p=\uu{P}(\omega_p,\uu{I})\) and
\(\uu{S}_s=\uu{S}(\omega_s,\uu{I})\) are the second-order Hill and
Eshelby tensors in the diffusion reference medium \(\uu{I}\).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ scipy.optimize }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ bisect}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ plotly.graph\_objects }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ go}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ felas(omega\_s, omega\_p):}
    \CommentTok{"""Elastic percolation: returns (f\_solid, nu) at C\_hom → 0+ (SC two{-}phase)."""}
\NormalTok{    ells }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ spheroidal(omega\_s, limit\_aspect\_ratio}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ellp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ spheroidal(omega\_p, limit\_aspect\_ratio}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{)}
    \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ solf(Ps, CImSp):}
\NormalTok{        tP }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.trace(J4.dot(Ps))}
\NormalTok{        tS }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.trace(J4.dot(CImSp))}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ tS }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (tP }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ tS)          }\CommentTok{\# solid fraction at threshold}
    \KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ eqnu(nu):}
\NormalTok{        C     }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, nu)}
\NormalTok{        Ps    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ isotropify(np.linalg.inv(hill(ells, C)))}
\NormalTok{        CImSp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C.array.dot(isotropify(np.linalg.inv(Id4 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ eshelby(ellp, C))))}
\NormalTok{        f     }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ solf(Ps, CImSp)}
\NormalTok{        tP }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.trace(K4.dot(Ps))}
\NormalTok{        tS }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.trace(K4.dot(CImSp))}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ f }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tP }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ tS}
\NormalTok{    nu    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ bisect(eqnu, }\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.4}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    C     }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, nu)}
\NormalTok{    Ps    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ isotropify(np.linalg.inv(hill(ells, C)))}
\NormalTok{    CImSp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C.array.dot(isotropify(np.linalg.inv(Id4 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ eshelby(ellp, C))))}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ solf(Ps, CImSp), nu}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ fdiff(omega\_s, omega\_p):}
    \CommentTok{"""Diffusion percolation: returns solid fraction f\_s at D\_hom → 0+ (SC two{-}phase).}
\CommentTok{    Pore percolation threshold = 1 {-} fdiff(...)."""}
\NormalTok{    ells }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ spheroidal(omega\_s, limit\_aspect\_ratio}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ellp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ spheroidal(omega\_p, limit\_aspect\_ratio}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    Pp   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ isotropify(np.linalg.inv(hill(ellp, tId2)))}
\NormalTok{    ImSs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ isotropify(np.linalg.inv(tId2.array }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ eshelby(ells, tId2)))}
\NormalTok{    tP }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.trace(Pp)}
\NormalTok{    tS }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.trace(ImSs)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ tP }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (tP }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ tS)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Grid: log10(ωs) × log10(ωp) ∈ [−3, 2]² — 51×51 points}
\NormalTok{nx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ny }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{51}
\NormalTok{logws }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{2.}\NormalTok{, nx)}
\NormalTok{logwp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{2.}\NormalTok{, ny)}
\NormalTok{Xg, Yg }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.meshgrid(logws, logwp, sparse}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, indexing}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}ij\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{Ze }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.zeros(Xg.shape)    }\CommentTok{\# elastic threshold (\% pores)}
\NormalTok{Zd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.zeros(Xg.shape)    }\CommentTok{\# diffusion threshold (\% pores)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(nx):}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ j }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(ny):}
\NormalTok{        f\_e, \_  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ felas(}\FloatTok{10.}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{Xg[i, j], }\FloatTok{10.}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{Yg[i, j])}
\NormalTok{        Ze[i, j] }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{100.} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f\_e)}
\NormalTok{        Zd[i, j] }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{100.} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fdiff(}\FloatTok{10.}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{Xg[i, j], }\FloatTok{10.}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{Yg[i, j]))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The maps below show \(\phi_e^{\rm elas}\) and \(\phi_e^{\rm diff}\) (in
\%) as functions of \(\log_{10}\omega_s\) and \(\log_{10}\omega_p\). The
calibration points of the engineering and detailed models are marked.

\NormalTok{        (np.log10(}\FloatTok{0.12}\NormalTok{),  np.log10(}\FloatTok{1.}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{0.12}\NormalTok{)),}
\NormalTok{\}}

\NormalTok{                            np.linspace(}\DecValTok{46}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{54}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{), np.linspace(}\DecValTok{55}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{95}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{                            [}\FloatTok{99.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{100.}\NormalTok{]))}
\NormalTok{levels\_d }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.concatenate(([}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{], np.linspace(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{                            np.linspace(}\DecValTok{21}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{33}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{), np.linspace(}\DecValTok{35}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{95}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{13}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{                            [}\FloatTok{99.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{100.}\NormalTok{]))}

\NormalTok{fig, (ax1, ax2) }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{))}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ ax, Z, levels, title }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{        (ax1, Ze, levels\_e, }\VerbatimStringTok{r"Elastic }\DecValTok{$}\ErrorTok{\textbackslash{}}\VerbatimStringTok{phi\_e}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}r}\VerbatimStringTok{m elas\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{\%}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{        (ax2, Zd, levels\_d, }\VerbatimStringTok{r"Diffusion }\DecValTok{$}\ErrorTok{\textbackslash{}}\VerbatimStringTok{phi\_e}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}r}\VerbatimStringTok{m diff\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{\%}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)]:}
\NormalTok{    cf }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ax.contourf(Xg, Yg, Z, levels)}
\NormalTok{    cl }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ax.contour(Xg, Yg, Z, cf.levels, colors}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}k\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, linestyles}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}solid\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.clabel(cl, inline}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, fmt}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\%2.0f\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{    ax.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\ErrorTok{\textbackslash{}}\VerbatimStringTok{log\_}\OperatorTok{\{10\}}\KeywordTok{(}\ErrorTok{\textbackslash{}}\VerbatimStringTok{omega\_p}\KeywordTok{)}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\begin{verbatim}
Unable to display output for mime type(s): text/html
\end{verbatim}

The diagrams confirm the calibration rationale: with oblate solid
(\(\omega_s \ll 1\)) and prolate pores (\(\omega_p \gg 1\)), the elastic
and diffusion percolation thresholds are well separated --- the solid
skeleton percolates at high porosity (early hydration, low \(\alpha\))
while the pore network remains connected until very low porosity (late
hydration). The engineering model calibration point
(\(\omega_s = 0.013\), \(\omega_p = 6\), marked by \(\star\)) sits in a
region where \(\phi_e^{\rm elas} \approx 15\,\%\) and
\(\phi_e^{\rm diff} \approx 50\,\%\), consistent with reported setting
degrees and with the experimentally observed diffusivity drop at
intermediate to high hydration (\citeproc{ref-achourCBM2020}{Achour et
al., 2020}).

\(\,\)

\chapter{Quasi-brittle strength of cement paste and
mortar}\label{sec-concrete-strength}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-important-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-important-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Objectives}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

This chapter implements the multi-scale strength upscaling model of
(\citeproc{ref-pichler2011}{Pichler and Hellmich, 2011}). It extends the
elastic cement paste model of Chapter~\ref{sec-concrete} with:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a \textbf{third scale} (mortar = cement paste + sand grains),
\item
  \textbf{anisotropic needle-shaped hydrates} with a random orientation
  distribution discretized over the hemisphere,
\item
  a \textbf{derivative-based strength criterion} (building on the
  framework of Chapter~\ref{sec-nonlinear}) that upscales the hydrate
  phase strength to the macroscopic compressive strength of the mortar.
\end{itemize}

\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Imports}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ numpy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ np}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ echoes }\ImportTok{import} \OperatorTok{*}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ math}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\NormalTok{np.set\_printoptions(precision}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, suppress}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Multi-scale microstructure model}\label{sec-pichler-model}

The model decomposes the mortar microstructure into three nested RVEs:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}cllc@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Scale & RVE & Phases & Scheme \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endfirsthead
\toprule\noalign{}
Scale & RVE & Phases & Scheme \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\tabularnewline
\caption{Three-scale RVE decomposition following
(\citeproc{ref-pichler2011}{Pichler and Hellmich,
2011}).}\label{tbl-scales}\tabularnewline
\endlastfoot
1 & Hydrate Foam (HF) & Hydrates (needles) + water + air pores & SC \\
2 & Cement Paste (CP) & HF matrix + clinker grains (spheres) & MT \\
3 & Mortar (MO) & CP matrix + sand grains (spheres) & MT \\
\end{longtable}

The key feature of this model --- compared to the simpler two-scale
model of Chapter~\ref{sec-concrete} --- is that the hydrate crystals at
scale 1 are represented as \textbf{needle-shaped inclusions} (prolate
spheroids with very large aspect ratio \(\omega \to \infty\)) with a
\textbf{random orientation distribution}. This captures the disordered
polycrystalline texture of the C-S-H gel.

\section{Volume fractions}\label{sec-pichler-volfracs}

The volume fractions of each phase at scales 1 and 2 follow from
stoichiometry (Powers model) as functions of the \textbf{water-to-cement
ratio} \(w/c\) and the \textbf{hydration degree}
\(\alpha \in [0,\,\alpha_{max}]\), where
\(\alpha_{max} = \min(1,\, w/c\,/\,0.42)\):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-volfracs-cp}{
\begin{aligned}
f_{clin}(\alpha) &= \frac{1-\alpha}{1+d_{clin}\,w/c} \\[4pt]
f_w(\alpha) &= \frac{d_{clin}(w/c - 0.42\,\alpha)}{1+d_{clin}\,w/c} \\[4pt]
f_{hyd}(\alpha) &= \frac{1.42\,d_{clin}/d_{hyd}\;\alpha}{1+d_{clin}\,w/c} \\[4pt]
f_{air} &= 1 - f_{clin} - f_w - f_{hyd}
\end{aligned}
}\end{equation}

The \textbf{sand volume fraction} in the mortar depends on the
sand-to-cement ratio \(s/c\):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-volfracs-mo}{
f_{sand} = \frac{(s/c)\,/\,d_{sand}}{1/d_{clin} + w/c + (s/c)\,/\,d_{sand}}
}\end{equation}

The phase densities relative to water are \(d_{clin}=3.15\),
\(d_{hyd}=2.073\), \(d_{sand}=2.648\).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Densities relative to water}
\NormalTok{d\_clin }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{3.15}
\NormalTok{d\_hyd  }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{2.073}
\NormalTok{d\_san  }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{2.648}

\CommentTok{\# Volume fractions (scales 1 \& 2)}
\NormalTok{f\_clin }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, alpha: (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ alpha) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ d\_clin }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ wc)}
\NormalTok{f\_w    }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, alpha: d\_clin }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (wc }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{0.42} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ alpha) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ d\_clin }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ wc)}
\NormalTok{f\_hyd  }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, alpha: }\FloatTok{1.42} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ d\_clin }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ d\_hyd }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ d\_clin }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ wc)}

\CommentTok{\# Sand volume fraction in mortar (scale 3)}
\NormalTok{f\_sand }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc, sc: (sc }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ d\_san) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ d\_clin }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ wc }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ sc }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ d\_san)}

\CommentTok{\# Maximum hydration degree}
\NormalTok{alpha\_max }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ wc: }\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, wc }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{0.42}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The elastic properties of each constituent are:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lrr@{}}

Phase & \(k\) (GPa) & \(\mu\) (GPa) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Clinker & 116.7 & 53.8 \\
Hydrates (C-S-H + portlandite) & 18.7 & 11.8 \\
Water & 0 & 0 \\
Air & 0 & 0 \\
Sand & 37.8 & 44.3 \\

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{C\_clin }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_kmu(}\FloatTok{116.7}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{53.8}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{C\_hyd  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_kmu(}\FloatTok{18.7}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{11.8}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{C\_w    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ tZ4                      }\CommentTok{\# zero stiffness}
\NormalTok{C\_air  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ tZ4                      }\CommentTok{\# zero stiffness}
\NormalTok{C\_san  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_kmu(}\FloatTok{37.8}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{44.3}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Three-scale elastic homogenization}\label{sec-pichler-elas}

\subsection{Needle-shaped hydrates and orientation
discretization}\label{sec-needle-orient}

The hydrate crystals are modeled as \textbf{prolate spheroids} with
aspect ratio \(\omega = 10^4\) (effectively needles). Their orientations
are assumed uniformly distributed over the unit sphere. By symmetry, the
orientation is parametrized by the polar angle
\(\theta \in [0,\, \pi/2]\) and the distribution is discretized into
\(n_\theta\) bins of equal solid-angle weight:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-orient-weight}{
f_{hyd}^{(i)} = f_{hyd} \cdot \bigl(\cos\theta_i^- - \cos\theta_i^+\bigr), \quad i = 0,\dots, n_\theta-1
}\end{equation}

where \(\theta_i^-\) and \(\theta_i^+\) are the lower and upper bounds
of bin \(i\), and \(\theta_i\) is its center. The hydrate foam RVE at
scale 1 contains \(n_\theta\) oriented inclusion families and is
homogenized with the \textbf{Self-Consistent (SC)} scheme:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-sc-hf}{
\mathbb{C}^{hom}_{HF} = \mathrm{SC}\!\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n_\theta-1} f_{hyd}^{(i)}\,\mathbb{C}_{hyd}^{(\theta_i)}
                            + f_w\,\mathbb{C}_w + f_{air}\,\mathbb{C}_{air}\right)
}\end{equation}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ disc\_theta(ntheta):}
    \CommentTok{"""Discretize [0, pi/2] into ntheta orientation bins.}
\CommentTok{    Returns (lower bounds, centers, upper bounds).}
\CommentTok{    """}
\NormalTok{    thm }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [np.pi }\OperatorTok{/} \DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (i }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (ntheta }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, ntheta)]}
\NormalTok{    th  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [np.pi }\OperatorTok{/} \DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (ntheta }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(ntheta)]}
\NormalTok{    thp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [np.pi }\OperatorTok{/} \DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (i }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (ntheta }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, ntheta }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [np.pi }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{2.}\NormalTok{]}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ thm, th, thp}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsection{Three-scale elastic model}\label{sec-3scale-elas}

At \textbf{scale 2} the cement paste is obtained by embedding clinker
grains in the hydrate foam matrix using \textbf{Mori-Tanaka (MT)}:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-mt-cp}{
\mathbb{C}^{hom}_{CP} = \mathrm{MT}_{HF}\bigl(f_{clin}\,\mathbb{C}_{clin}\bigr)
}\end{equation}

At \textbf{scale 3} the mortar is obtained by embedding sand grains in
the cement paste matrix, again using MT:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-mt-mo}{
\mathbb{C}^{hom}_{MO} = \mathrm{MT}_{CP}\bigl(f_{sand}\,\mathbb{C}_{sand}\bigr)
}\end{equation}

\section{Strength upscaling via derivative-based
criterion}\label{sec-pichler-strength}

\subsection{Theoretical framework}\label{sec-strength-theory}

The macroscopic \textbf{uniaxial compressive strength} \(f_c\) of the
mortar is upscaled from the \textbf{phase-level strength}
\(\sigma^{ult}_{hyd}\) of the hydrates using the sensitivity of the
effective stiffness to the hydrate shear modulus --- a direct
application of the framework in Chapter~\ref{sec-nonlinear}.

The \textbf{compliance sensitivity matrix} with respect to the shear
modulus \(\mu_{hyd}\) of hydrate orientation bin \(\theta\) is:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-M-sens}{
\mathbf{M}^{(\theta)} = \mathbf{S}^{hom}_{MO} \cdot \frac{\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{MO}}{\partial \mu_{hyd}^{(\theta)}} \cdot \mathbf{S}^{hom}_{MO}
}\end{equation}

where \(\mathbf{S}^{hom}_{MO} = \bigl(\mathbf{C}^{hom}_{MO}\bigr)^{-1}\)
is the effective compliance, and all quantities are \(6\times 6\)
matrices in Kelvin-Mandel notation.

For uniaxial compression along \(\uu{e}_3\), the macroscopic strength
normalized by \(\sigma^{ult}_{hyd}\) reads
(\citeproc{ref-pichler2011}{Pichler and Hellmich, 2011}):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-fc}{
\boxed{\frac{f_c}{\sigma^{ult}_{hyd}} = \min_\theta \sqrt{\frac{f^{(\theta)}_{hyd}}{2\,\mu_{hyd}^2\,M^{(\theta)}_{33}}}}
}\end{equation}

where \(M_{33}\) is the \((3,3)\) entry of \(\mathbf{M}^{(\theta)}\)
(compression axis), and

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-fhyd-abs}{
f^{(\theta)}_{hyd} = f_{hyd}^{(i)} \cdot (1 - f_{clin}) \cdot (1 - f_{sand})
}\end{equation}

is the absolute volume fraction of hydrates in orientation bin
\(\theta\) within the full mortar. The factor \(2\mu_{hyd}^2\) converts
the stiffness sensitivity into a deviatoric stress scale at the hydrate
level.

\subsection{Chain rule across three scales}\label{sec-chain-rule}

The derivative
\(\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{MO} / \partial \mu_{hyd}^{(\theta)}\)
propagates through three homogenization steps. It is assembled via the
\textbf{chain rule}:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-chain}{
\frac{\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{MO}}{\partial \mu_{hyd}^{(\theta)}}
= \sum_{\alpha=0}^{4}\sum_{\beta=0}^{4}
  \frac{\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{MO}}{\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{CP}[\alpha]}
  \cdot
  \left(\frac{\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{CP}}{\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{HF}[\beta]}\right)[\alpha]
  \cdot
  \left(\frac{\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{HF}}{\partial \mu_{hyd}^{(\theta)}}\right)[\beta]
}\end{equation}

where indices \(\alpha, \beta \in \{0,\dots,4\}\) enumerate the five
independent parameters of the transversely isotropic (TI) effective
tensors \(\mathbb{C}^{hom}_{HF}\) and \(\mathbb{C}^{hom}_{CP}\). Each
factor is obtained by one \texttt{homogenize\_derivative} call per
index:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2222}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2778}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Factor
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\texttt{phase}
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\texttt{index}
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
\texttt{scheme}
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\(\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{HF}/\partial\mu_{hyd}^{(\theta)}\) &
\texttt{"HYD0"} & \texttt{1} (TI: transverse \(\mu\)) & \texttt{SC} on
\texttt{rve2\_hf} \\
\(\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{CP}/\partial\mathbf{C}^{hom}_{HF}[\beta]\)
& \texttt{"HF"} & \texttt{beta} & \texttt{MT} on \texttt{rve\_cp} \\
\(\partial \mathbf{C}^{hom}_{MO}/\partial\mathbf{C}^{hom}_{CP}[\alpha]\)
& \texttt{"CP"} & \texttt{alpha} & \texttt{MT} on \texttt{rve\_mo} \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Complete homogenization and strength
function}\label{sec-homo-fn}

The following function assembles all three scales (elastic + strength)
in a single call.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ homo(wc, alpha}\OperatorTok{={-}}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{, sc}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, omega}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{10000.}\NormalTok{, ntheta}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{):}
    \CommentTok{"""Three{-}scale homogenization of mortar: elastic stiffness + normalized strength.}

\CommentTok{    Parameters}
\CommentTok{    {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{    wc    : water{-}to{-}cement ratio}
\CommentTok{    alpha : hydration degree (default: alpha\_max)}
\CommentTok{    sc    : sand{-}to{-}cement ratio (0 = pure cement paste)}
\CommentTok{    omega : needle aspect ratio}
\CommentTok{    ntheta: number of orientation bins}

\CommentTok{    Returns}
\CommentTok{    {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{    [True, C\_mo, fc]  — C\_mo: effective stiffness; fc: f\_c / sigma\_hyd\^{}ult}
\CommentTok{    [False]           — computation failed (e.g. negative water volume fraction)}
\CommentTok{    """}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        alpha }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ alpha\_max(wc)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ alpha }\OperatorTok{==} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{:}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ [}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, tensor([}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]), }\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{    fclin }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f\_clin(wc, alpha)}
\NormalTok{    fw    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f\_w(wc, alpha)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{   ftw   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fw   }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fclin)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ fw }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{:}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ [}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{    fhyd\_val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f\_hyd(wc, alpha)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ fthyd }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fhyd\_val }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fclin)}
\NormalTok{    fair     }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fclin }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fw }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fhyd\_val}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{ ftair }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fair }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fclin)}
\NormalTok{    fhsan    }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f\_sand(wc, sc)}

    \CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
    \CommentTok{\# Scale 1 — Hydrate Foam: fast isotropic SC for convergence}
    \CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{    rve\_hf }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
\NormalTok{    rve\_hf[}\StringTok{"HYD"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                               fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{fthyd, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_hyd\})}
\NormalTok{    rve\_hf[}\StringTok{"W"}\NormalTok{]   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ftw,   prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_w\})}
\NormalTok{    rve\_hf[}\StringTok{"AIR"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ftair, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_air\})}
\NormalTok{    C\_hf }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rve\_hf, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                      verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ math.isnan(C\_hf.k) }\KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ math.isnan(C\_hf.mu):}
        \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ [}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{]}

    \CommentTok{\# Orientation{-}resolved HF RVE (ntheta bins, TI needles)}
\NormalTok{    thm, theta, thp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ disc\_theta(ntheta)}
\NormalTok{    rve2\_hf }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve()}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(ntheta):}
\NormalTok{        rve2\_hf[}\StringTok{"HYD"} \OperatorTok{+} \BuiltInTok{str}\NormalTok{(i)] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(}
\NormalTok{            shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spheroidal(omega, theta[i]), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[TI],}
\NormalTok{            fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{fthyd }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (np.cos(thm[i]) }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ np.cos(thp[i])),}
\NormalTok{            prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_hyd\})}
\NormalTok{    rve2\_hf[}\StringTok{"W"}\NormalTok{]   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ftw,   prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_w\})}
\NormalTok{    rve2\_hf[}\StringTok{"AIR"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ftair, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_air\})}
    \CommentTok{\# Seed with converged C\_hf; run 1 SC step to update oriented{-}needle Eshelby tensors}
\NormalTok{    rve2\_hf.set\_prop(}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, C\_hf)}
\NormalTok{    homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rve2\_hf, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{               verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}

    \CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
    \CommentTok{\# Scale 2 — Cement Paste: MT with HF matrix}
    \CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{    rve\_cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"HF"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    rve\_cp[}\StringTok{"HF"}\NormalTok{]   }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fclin,}
\NormalTok{                                prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tensor(C\_hf.array, TI)\})}
\NormalTok{    rve\_cp[}\StringTok{"CLIN"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{fclin,}
\NormalTok{                                prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_clin\})}
\NormalTok{    C\_cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rve\_cp, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}

    \CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
    \CommentTok{\# Scale 3 — Mortar: MT with CP matrix}
    \CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{    rve\_mo }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"CP"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    rve\_mo[}\StringTok{"CP"}\NormalTok{]  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fhsan,}
\NormalTok{                               prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: tensor(C\_cp.array, TI)\})}
\NormalTok{    rve\_mo[}\StringTok{"SAN"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{fhsan,}
\NormalTok{                               prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C\_san\})}
\NormalTok{    C\_mo }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rve\_mo, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}

    \CommentTok{\# ================================================================}
    \CommentTok{\# Derivative chain rule for strength (eq. \{\#eq{-}chain\})}
    \CommentTok{\# ================================================================}
\NormalTok{    rve2\_hf.set\_param\_eshelby(algo}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{NUMINT, epsroots}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                               epsabs}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100000}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    rve\_cp.set\_param\_eshelby(algo}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{NUMINT, epsroots}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                              epsabs}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100000}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    rve\_mo.set\_param\_eshelby(algo}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{NUMINT, epsroots}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                              epsabs}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}3}\NormalTok{, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100000}\NormalTok{)}

    \CommentTok{\# Scale 3: dC\_mo / dC\_cp[alpha]  (alpha = 0..4, TI parameters of CP)}
\NormalTok{    dC\_mo\_dC\_cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{        homogenize\_derivative(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rve\_mo, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT,}
\NormalTok{                               phase}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"CP"}\NormalTok{, index}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{i, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{).array}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ]}

    \CommentTok{\# Scale 2: dC\_cp / dC\_hf[beta]  (beta = 0..4, TI parameters of HF)}
\NormalTok{    dC\_cp\_dC\_hf }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{        homogenize\_derivative(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rve\_cp, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT,}
\NormalTok{                               phase}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"HF"}\NormalTok{, index}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{i, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{).paramsym(sym}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{TI)}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ]}

    \CommentTok{\# Scale 1: dC\_hf / dmu\_hyd\^{}(theta=0)  (horizontal needles, index=1 = transverse mu\_T)}
    \CommentTok{\# The general loop over all bins (range(ntheta)) is commented out; only the}
    \CommentTok{\# most critical orientation (theta=0, bin 0) is used here.}
\NormalTok{    dC\_hf\_dmutheta }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{        homogenize\_derivative(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rve2\_hf, scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{SC,}
\NormalTok{                               phase}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"HYD0"}\NormalTok{, index}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, sym}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{TI, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{).paramsym(sym}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{TI)}
\NormalTok{    ]  }\CommentTok{\# one entry per orientation evaluated; generalize to range(ntheta) for full minimization}

    \CommentTok{\# Assemble chain rule and compute strength for each orientation bin}
\NormalTok{    fc\_vals }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ itheta, dC\_hf\_dmu }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{enumerate}\NormalTok{(dC\_hf\_dmutheta):}
        \CommentTok{\# Double sum over TI parameter indices}
\NormalTok{        dC }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{            [dC\_mo\_dC\_cp[a] }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dC\_cp\_dC\_hf[b][a] }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ dC\_hf\_dmu[b]}
             \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ b }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{) }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ a }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{)],}
\NormalTok{            np.zeros((}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{        )}
        \CommentTok{\# Compliance sensitivity: M = S\_hom . dC . S\_hom  (eq. \{\#eq{-}M{-}sens\})}
\NormalTok{        Shom }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linalg.inv(C\_mo.array)}
\NormalTok{        M }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ Shom.dot(dC.dot(Shom))}

        \CommentTok{\# Absolute hydrate volume fraction for this bin in the full mortar}
\NormalTok{        f }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve2\_hf[}\StringTok{"HYD"} \OperatorTok{+} \BuiltInTok{str}\NormalTok{(itheta)].fraction }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fclin) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ fhsan)}

        \CommentTok{\# Normalized strength (eq. \{\#eq{-}fc\}): M[2,2] = M\_33 in 0{-}based indexing}
\NormalTok{        fc\_val }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ np.sqrt(M[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ C\_hyd.mu}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ f)}
\NormalTok{        fc\_vals.append(fc\_val)}

\NormalTok{    fc }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(fc\_vals) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ fc\_vals }\ControlFlowTok{else} \FloatTok{0.}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ math.isnan(fc):}
\NormalTok{        fc }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ [}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, C\_mo, fc]}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Note}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

\textbf{Why orientation bin 0?} Bin 0 corresponds to \(\theta_0 = 0\),
i.e.~needles lying \textbf{perpendicular to the compression axis}
\(\uu{e}_3\). For a uniaxial compressive loading this orientation
maximizes \(M_{33}\) and therefore minimizes the strength \(f_c\),
making it the critical orientation. The general procedure is to evaluate
all \(n_\theta\) bins and take the minimum, as indicated by the
commented-out \texttt{range(ntheta)} in the original script.

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Results}\label{sec-pichler-results}

The following code computes the evolution of the effective stiffness
moduli (\(k\), \(\mu\)) and the normalized compressive strength
\(f_c/\sigma^{ult}_{hyd}\) as a function of the hydration degree
\(\alpha\) for several water-to-cement ratios \(w/c\), for pure cement
paste (\(s/c=0\)).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{wc\_list }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.157}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.25}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.35}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.50}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.65}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.80}\NormalTok{]}

\CommentTok{\# ── Figure 1 : évolution avec α (pâte de ciment, s/c = 0) ────────────────}
\NormalTok{fig }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{6.5}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{gs  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fig.add\_gridspec(}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, hspace}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.45}\NormalTok{, wspace}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.35}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax\_k  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fig.add\_subplot(gs[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{])}
\NormalTok{ax\_mu }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fig.add\_subplot(gs[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{])}
\NormalTok{ax\_fc }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fig.add\_subplot(gs[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{])}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ wc }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ wc\_list:}
\NormalTok{    lalpha, lk, lmu, lfc }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [], [], [], []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ alpha }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(wc }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{0.42}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{), }\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{        res }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homo(wc, alpha, sc}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ res[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{            lalpha.append(alpha)}
\NormalTok{            lk.append(res[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].k)}
\NormalTok{            lmu.append(res[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].mu)}
\NormalTok{            lfc.append(res[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{])}
\NormalTok{    label }\OperatorTok{=} \SpecialStringTok{f"w/c = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{wc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}
\NormalTok{    ax\_k.plot(lalpha,  lk,  marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}+\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, label}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{label)}
\NormalTok{    ax\_mu.plot(lalpha, lmu, marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}+\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, label}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{label)}
\NormalTok{    ax\_fc.plot(lalpha, lfc, marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}+\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, label}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{label)}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ ax, ylabel }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [(ax\_k,  }\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{k}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{GPa}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{                   (ax\_mu, }\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$\textbackslash{}m}\VerbatimStringTok{u}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{hom\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ }\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{GPa}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{                   (ax\_fc, }\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{f\_c}\CharTok{\textbackslash{},}\VerbatimStringTok{/}\CharTok{\textbackslash{},}\DecValTok{\textbackslash{}s}\VerbatimStringTok{igma}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{ult\}\_\{hyd\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)]:}
\NormalTok{    ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"Hydration degree }\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}a}\VerbatimStringTok{lpha}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.set\_ylabel(ylabel)}
\NormalTok{    ax.set\_xlim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, ls}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}{-}{-}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, alpha}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.legend(fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.suptitle(}\StringTok{"Cement paste ($s/c = 0$)"}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{11}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}

\CommentTok{\# ── Figure 2 : effet de s/c sur la résistance (α = α\_max) ────────────────}
\NormalTok{fig2, ax2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{))}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ wc }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.35}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.50}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.65}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{    lsc, lfc }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [], []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ sc }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ np.linspace(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{5.}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{        res }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homo(wc, sc}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{sc)}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ res[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{] }\KeywordTok{and}\NormalTok{ res[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{            lsc.append(sc)}
\NormalTok{            lfc.append(res[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{])}
\NormalTok{    ax2.plot(lsc, lfc, marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}o\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"w/c = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{wc}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax2.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"Sand{-}to{-}cement ratio }\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{s/c}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax2.set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{f\_c}\CharTok{\textbackslash{},}\VerbatimStringTok{/}\CharTok{\textbackslash{},}\DecValTok{\textbackslash{}s}\VerbatimStringTok{igma}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{ult\}\_\{hyd\}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax2.set\_xlim(}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{5.}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax2.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, ls}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}{-}{-}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, alpha}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ax2.legend()}
\NormalTok{ax2.set\_title(}\VerbatimStringTok{r"Effect of }\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{s/c}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{ on normalized strength }\KeywordTok{(}\DecValTok{$}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}a}\VerbatimStringTok{lpha = }\CharTok{\textbackslash{}a}\VerbatimStringTok{lpha\_\{max\}}\DecValTok{$}\KeywordTok{)}\VerbatimStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/concrete_strength_files/figure-pdf/cell-7-output-1.pdf}}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/concrete_strength_files/figure-pdf/cell-7-output-2.pdf}}

\textbf{Figure 1} shows that the normalized strength
\(f_c/\sigma^{ult}_{hyd}\) increases monotonically with hydration degree
and decreases with \(w/c\), consistent with the experimental trends
reported in (\citeproc{ref-pichler2011}{Pichler and Hellmich, 2011}).
The absolute compressive strength (MPa) is recovered by multiplying by
\(\sigma^{ult}_{hyd}\), which (\citeproc{ref-pichler2011}{Pichler and
Hellmich, 2011}) calibrate to approximately 70--90 MPa for typical
C-S-H.

\textbf{Figure 2} illustrates the effect of the sand content on the
normalized strength at full hydration (\(\alpha = \alpha_{max}\)).
Increasing \(s/c\) dilutes the hydrate foam and reduces the effective
strength, with the effect being more pronounced at lower \(w/c\) ratios.

\(\,\)

\chapter{Ageing creep of solidifying cementitious
materials}\label{sec-ageing-creep}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-important-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-important-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Objectives}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

This chapter implements the ageing creep homogenization model of
(\citeproc{ref-sanahuja2013a}{J. Sanahuja, 2013}), building on the
time-domain framework introduced in
Chapter~\ref{sec-viscoelasticity-time}. The model represents a composite
where one phase solidifies progressively from a pore to a viscoelastic
solid --- as occurs in cementitious materials during hydration. The
chapter illustrates:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Ageing viscoelastic homogenization} via
  \texttt{homogenize\_visco} for materials whose properties depend on
  both observation time \(t\) and loading time \(t'\),
\item
  \textbf{Solidification discretization} into \(N\) layers with
  individual setting times,
\item
  Two RVE models:
  \texttt{\textquotesingle{}whole\ pores\textquotesingle{}} (N separate
  inclusions) vs \texttt{\textquotesingle{}layers\textquotesingle{}}
  (one \texttt{sphere\_nlayers}) --- the latter being the efficient
  approach of (\citeproc{ref-sanahuja2013a}{J. Sanahuja, 2013}),
\item
  \textbf{History-dependent} vs \textbf{frozen} loading-time approaches.
\end{itemize}

\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title={Imports}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]

\section{Ageing viscoelasticity}\label{sec-ageing-visco}

For a \textbf{non-ageing} viscoelastic composite, the relaxation tensor
depends only on the elapsed time \(t - t'\):

\[\mathbb{R}(t,t') = \mathbb{R}(t - t')\]

and the standard Laplace--Carson correspondence principle applies (see
Chapter~\ref{sec-complex-moduli}). For an \textbf{ageing} composite ---
where the microstructure itself evolves --- \(\mathbb{R}(t,t')\) depends
on both the current time \(t\) and the loading time \(t'\)
independently, breaking the convolution structure:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-ageing-constitutive}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}(t) = \int_{-\infty}^{t} \mathbb{R}(t,t') : \mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(t')
}\end{equation}

The effective ageing relaxation tensor \(\mathbb{R}^{hom}(t,t')\) must
then be computed directly in the time domain, without recourse to the
Laplace transform. This is precisely the setting of
\texttt{homogenize\_visco} in \emph{Echoes} (see
Chapter~\ref{sec-viscoelasticity-time}): any phase whose
\texttt{visco\_prop} is set to a two-argument function
\texttt{func(t,\ tp)} is treated as ageing-viscoelastic.

\section{Phase properties}\label{sec-ageing-phases}

The composite has three phases:
