Matrix & \(f_0 = 0.6\) & \(E_0=1\), \(\nu_0=0.2\) & Maxwell \\
Solidifying inclusions (final) & \(f_\infty = 0.3\) & \(E_1=5\),
\(\nu_1=0.3\) & Maxwell \\
Pore (pre-solidified state) & \(f_p = 1-f_0-f_\infty\) &
\(E_p \approx 0\) & elastic \\
\end{longtable}

Both the matrix and the solidifying phase obey a \textbf{Maxwell}
relaxation law with separate bulk and shear characteristic times:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-maxwell}{R_r(t,t') = 3k_r\,e^{-(t-t')/\eta_r}\,\mathbb{J} + 2\mu_r\,e^{-(t-t')/\gamma_r}\,\mathbb{K}
}\end{equation}

where \(\mathbb{J} = \frac{1}{3}\uv{1}\otimes\uv{1}\) and
\(\mathbb{K} = \mathbb{I} - \mathbb{J}\) are the volumetric and
deviatoric projectors.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lcc@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Matrix (\(r=0\)) & Solidifying (\(r=1\)) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\(k_r\) (bulk) & \(k_0 = C_0.k\) & \(k_1 = C_1.k\) \\
\(\eta_r\) (bulk char. time) & \(0.2\) & \(1.0\) \\
\(\mu_r\) (shear) & \(\mu_0 = C_0.\mu\) & \(\mu_1 = C_1.\mu\) \\
\(\gamma_r\) (shear char. time) & \(0.133\) & \(1.67\) \\
\end{longtable}
}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Elastic stiffness tensors}
\NormalTok{E0 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  nu0 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.2}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  C0 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(E0, nu0)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  f0 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.6}
\NormalTok{E1 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{5.}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  nu1 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.3}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  C1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(E1, nu1)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  finf }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.3}
\NormalTok{Ep }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ E0 }\OperatorTok{*} \FloatTok{1.e{-}8}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{      Cp }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ stiff\_Enu(Ep, nu0)}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  fp }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f0 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ finf}

\NormalTok{eta0 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.2}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{   gamma0 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.133}
\NormalTok{eta1 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{    gamma1 }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1.67}

\CommentTok{\# Maxwell relaxation tensors (eq. \{\#eq{-}maxwell\})}
\CommentTok{\# J4, K4 are the 6x6 Kelvin{-}Mandel volumetric and deviatoric projectors}
\NormalTok{R0 }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ t, tp: }\DecValTok{3} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ C0.k }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{(t }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ tp) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ eta0)   }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ J4 }\OperatorTok{\textbackslash{}}
                 \OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ C0.mu }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{(t }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ tp) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ gamma0) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ K4}
\NormalTok{R1 }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ t, tp: }\DecValTok{3} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ C1.k }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{(t }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ tp) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ eta1)   }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ J4 }\OperatorTok{\textbackslash{}}
                 \OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ C1.mu }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{(t }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ tp) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ gamma1) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ K4}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Solidification kinetics}\label{sec-solidification}

The volume fraction of the solidified phase at time \(t\) follows a
power-law growth:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-solidification}{f_{solid}(t) = f_\infty\,\frac{t^\alpha}{1+t^\alpha}
}\end{equation}

which tends to \(f_\infty\) as \(t \to \infty\). The exponent \(\alpha\)
controls the rate of solidification. The inverse function --- the time
at which a fraction \(f\) of the solidifying phase has set --- is:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-setting-time}{t_k(f) = \left(\frac{f}{f_\infty - f}\right)^{1/\alpha}
}\end{equation}

To discretize this continuous process into \(N\) layers of equal volume
\(f_\infty/N\), each layer \(k\) is assigned the setting time
corresponding to the midpoint fraction:

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-layers}{f_k = \left(k + \tfrac{1}{2}\right)\frac{f_\infty}{N}, \quad
t_k = t_k(f_k), \quad k = 0, \ldots, N-1
}\end{equation}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ft  }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ t, alpha: finf }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ t}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{alpha }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ t}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{alpha)   }\CommentTok{\# eq. \{\#eq{-}solidification\}}
\NormalTok{tf  }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ f, alpha: (f }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (finf }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ f))}\OperatorTok{**}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ alpha)     }\CommentTok{\# eq. \{\#eq{-}setting{-}time\}}
\NormalTok{F   }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ N: np.array([(i }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ finf }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ N }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N)])   }\CommentTok{\# midpoint fractions}
\NormalTok{tT  }\OperatorTok{=} \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ N, alpha: tf(F(N), alpha)                    }\CommentTok{\# setting times for N layers}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{RVE construction}\label{sec-ageing-rve-construction}

\subsection{`whole pores' model}\label{sec-whole-pores}

In this model, each of the \(N\) solidifying layers is a
\textbf{separate ellipsoidal inclusion} in the RVE. The RVE contains
\(N + 2\) phases: the permanent matrix, a single pore inclusion
(fraction \(f_p\)), and \(N\) solidifying inclusions (each of fraction
\(f_\infty/N\)).

This is conceptually straightforward but computationally expensive for
large \(N\): each inclusion requires a separate Eshelby tensor
computation.

\subsection{`layers' model --- efficient
approach}\label{sec-layers-model}

The key contribution of (\citeproc{ref-sanahuja2013a}{J. Sanahuja,
2013}) is to pack all layers into a single \textbf{multi-layer sphere}
(\texttt{sphere\_nlayers}), with the pore as the outermost shell and the
\(N\) solidifying layers inside (ordered by increasing setting time
toward the center). The RVE reduces to 2 phases: MATRIX + one
sphere\_nlayers inclusion.

This reduces the \(N+1\) Eshelby problems of the `whole pores' model to
\textbf{a single problem} --- a substantial efficiency gain for
\(N = 100\) layers.

\begin{verbatim}
sphere_nlayers(radius=1.,
    layer_fractions=[fp, finf/N, finf/N, ..., finf/N],   # outermost = pore
    fraction=fp + finf,
    prop={"C": [Cp, C1(t0), C1(t0), ..., C1(t0)]},
    visco_prop={"C": [R_pore, R_layer_0, R_layer_1, ..., R_layer_{N-1}]})
\end{verbatim}

(Innermost layers: set earliest, index \(N-1-i\) → latest setting time
outward.)

\subsection{History-dependent vs frozen approach}\label{sec-history}

The central physical question is: \textbf{does a solidifying layer carry
memory of stress applied before it solidified?}

\textbf{History-dependent} (\texttt{fixed=False}): the relaxation
function of layer \(i\) checks the \emph{loading time} \(t'\). A layer
responds as a solid only if it had already solidified at the loading
time \(t'\):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-history-dep}{R_i(t,t') = \begin{cases} R_1(t,t') & \text{if } t' \geq t_k^{(i)} \\ \mathbf{C}_p & \text{if } t' < t_k^{(i)} \end{cases}
}\end{equation}

This is physically correct: the newly formed solid is deposited in a
stress-free state and begins to creep only from the moment it sets.

\textbf{Frozen} (\texttt{fixed=True}): the relaxation function uses the
\emph{start of the observation window} \(t_0\) instead of \(t'\). All
layers that had set by \(t_0\) are treated as having been present (and
loading) since \(t_0\):

\begin{equation}\protect\phantomsection\label{eq-frozen}{R_i(t,t') = \begin{cases} R_1(t,t') & \text{if } t_0 \geq t_k^{(i)} \\ \mathbf{C}_p & \text{if } t_0 < t_k^{(i)} \end{cases}
}\end{equation}

The frozen approach is a computationally identical but physically
approximate model that neglects the history before \(t_0\).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ build\_rve(N, alpha, t0, model}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}whole pores\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fixed}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{):}
    \CommentTok{"""Build the RVE for the solidifying composite.}

\CommentTok{    Parameters}
\CommentTok{    {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{    N     : number of solidification layers}
\CommentTok{    alpha : solidification exponent}
\CommentTok{    t0    : start of observation window (= loading time for this RVE)}
\CommentTok{    model : \textquotesingle{}whole pores\textquotesingle{} (N separate inclusions) or \textquotesingle{}layers\textquotesingle{} (sphere\_nlayers)}
\CommentTok{    fixed : False = history{-}dependent; True = frozen at t0}
\CommentTok{    """}
\NormalTok{    lT  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ tT(N, alpha)       }\CommentTok{\# setting times of each layer}
\NormalTok{    ver }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rve(matrix}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"MATRIX"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ver[}\StringTok{"MATRIX"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(spherical, fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{f0,}
\NormalTok{                               prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C0\},}
\NormalTok{                               visco\_prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: (R0, RELAXATION)\})}

    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ model }\OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}whole pores\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        ver[}\StringTok{"PORE"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(spheroidal(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO],}
\NormalTok{                                fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{fp, prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: Cp\})}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N):}
            \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ fixed:}
                \CommentTok{\# Frozen: condition on t0 (state of microstructure at start)}
\NormalTok{                ver[}\StringTok{"INCLUSION"} \OperatorTok{+} \BuiltInTok{str}\NormalTok{(i)] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(}
\NormalTok{                    spheroidal(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO], fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{finf }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ N,}
\NormalTok{                    prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C1 }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lT[i] }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp\},}
\NormalTok{                    visco\_prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: (}
                        \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ t, tp, lt}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{lT[i]: R1(t, tp) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lt }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp.array,}
\NormalTok{                        RELAXATION)\})}
            \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{:}
                \CommentTok{\# History{-}dependent: condition on loading time tp (eq. \{\#eq{-}history{-}dep\})}
\NormalTok{                ver[}\StringTok{"INCLUSION"} \OperatorTok{+} \BuiltInTok{str}\NormalTok{(i)] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ellipsoid(}
\NormalTok{                    spheroidal(}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{), symmetrize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[ISO], fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{finf }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ N,}
\NormalTok{                    prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: C1 }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lT[i] }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp\},}
\NormalTok{                    visco\_prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: (}
                        \KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ t, tp, lt}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{lT[i]: R1(t, tp) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ tp }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lt }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp.array,}
\NormalTok{                        RELAXATION)\})}
    \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{:  }\CommentTok{\# \textquotesingle{}layers\textquotesingle{} — efficient sphere\_nlayers}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ fixed:}
\NormalTok{            ver[}\StringTok{"INCLUSION"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(}
\NormalTok{                radius}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                layer\_fractions}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[fp] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [finf }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ N }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ \_ }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N)],}
\NormalTok{                fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{finf }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fp,}
\NormalTok{                prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: [Cp] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [C1 }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lT[N }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ i] }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp}
                                   \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N)]\},}
\NormalTok{                visco\_prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{                    [(}\KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ \_t, \_tp: Cp.array, RELAXATION)] }\OperatorTok{+}
\NormalTok{                    [(}\KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ t, tp, lt}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{lT[N }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ i]: R1(t, tp) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lt }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp.array,}
\NormalTok{                      RELAXATION)}
                     \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N)]\})}
        \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{            ver[}\StringTok{"INCLUSION"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sphere\_nlayers(}
\NormalTok{                radius}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                layer\_fractions}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[fp] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [finf }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ N }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ \_ }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N)],}
\NormalTok{                fraction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{finf }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ fp,}
\NormalTok{                prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{: [Cp] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [C1 }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ t0 }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lT[N }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ i] }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp}
                                   \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N)]\},}
\NormalTok{                visco\_prop}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{\{}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{                    [(}\KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ \_t, \_tp: Cp.array, RELAXATION)] }\OperatorTok{+}
\NormalTok{                    [(}\KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ t, tp, lt}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{lT[N }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ i]: R1(t, tp) }\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ tp }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=}\NormalTok{ lt }\ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{ Cp.array,}
\NormalTok{                      RELAXATION)}
                     \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(N)]\})}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ ver}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{Time-domain homogenization}\label{sec-visco-homo}

The \texttt{homogenize\_visco} function discretizes the constitutive law
\ref{eq-ageing-constitutive} over a time series
\(T = [T_0, T_1, \ldots, T_n]\) using a Stieltjes collocation scheme
(see Chapter~\ref{sec-viscoelasticity-time}). It returns the block
stiffness matrix \(\mathbf{R}^{hom}\) of size
\((n+1)\times 6 \,\times\, (n+1)\times 6\), where block \((i,j)\)
encodes \(\mathbb{R}^{hom}(T_i, T_j)\):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\_hom }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ homogenize\_visco(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, time\_series}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{T,}
\NormalTok{                          scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Inverting \(\mathbf{R}^{hom}\) gives the effective \textbf{creep
compliance} matrix \(\mathbf{J}^{hom} = (\mathbf{R}^{hom})^{-1}\). The
effective uniaxial creep function \(E_0\,J^{eff}_E(t,t_0)\) is then
extracted from the diagonal blocks.

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, arc=.35mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, breakable, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, coltitle=black, left=2mm, leftrule=.75mm, opacityback=0, opacitybacktitle=0.6, rightrule=.15mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Note}, titlerule=0mm, toprule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm]

\textbf{No correspondence principle needed.} Because
\(\mathbb{R}^{hom}(t,t')\) depends on both time arguments independently,
the standard Laplace--Carson approach is inapplicable. The direct
time-domain discretization of \texttt{homogenize\_visco} handles this
naturally, at the cost of solving a full
\((n+1)\cdot 6 \times (n+1)\cdot 6\) linear system.

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Results}\label{sec-ageing-results}

The \texttt{Jhom} function computes the effective creep compliance for
both the history-dependent and frozen RVEs, then extracts
\(E_0 J^{eff}_E\) by summing the isotropic diagonal blocks of the
inverted matrix.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ Jhom(T, N, alpha, model}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}whole pores\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{):}
    \CommentTok{"""Compute effective creep compliance for history{-}dependent and frozen RVEs."""}
    \CommentTok{\# History{-}dependent RVE (fixed=False)}
\NormalTok{    ver  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ build\_rve(N, alpha, T[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{], model, fixed}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    V }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linalg.inv(}
\NormalTok{        homogenize\_visco(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver, time\_series}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{T,}
\NormalTok{                         scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{))}
    \CommentTok{\# Frozen RVE (fixed=True)}
\NormalTok{    ver2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ build\_rve(N, alpha, T[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{], model, fixed}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    W }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.linalg.inv(}
\NormalTok{        homogenize\_visco(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{, rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ver2, time\_series}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{T,}
\NormalTok{                         scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}6}\NormalTok{, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{))}
    \CommentTok{\# Extract E0*J\^{}eff\_E: sum the "11" diagonal blocks over loading times}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(V[::}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, ::}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{], }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{), np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(W[::}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, ::}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{], }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The outer loop sweeps over five \textbf{loading ages} \(t_0\) (expressed
in units of the matrix shear characteristic time \(\gamma_0\)). For each
\(t_0\), the observation window is \([t_0,\, 10/3]\) on a log-spaced
grid. Both the \texttt{\textquotesingle{}layers\textquotesingle{}} and
\texttt{\textquotesingle{}whole\ pores\textquotesingle{}} models are
computed side by side to verify their equivalence.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{N }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{100}\OperatorTok{;}\NormalTok{  alpha }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{4.}

\NormalTok{fig, axes }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{), sharey}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ ax, model }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{zip}\NormalTok{(axes, [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}layers\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}whole pores\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]):}
    \CommentTok{\# Elastic reference: 1/E\^{}hom(t) at each solidification time step}
\NormalTok{    T\_el }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ tT(N, alpha).tolist() }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{10.} \OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{    E\_el }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{1.} \OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ homogenize(prop}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"C"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                             rve}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{build\_rve(N, alpha, t, model),}
\NormalTok{                             maxnb}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1000}\NormalTok{, epsrel}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.e{-}10}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                             scheme}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{MT, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{).E}
            \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ T\_el]}
\NormalTok{    ax.plot(T\_el, E\_el, color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}k\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, linestyle}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dotted\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, label}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}elastic $1/E\^{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{hom\}}\StringTok{$\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t0 }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ [}\FloatTok{1.}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{2.}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{4.}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{2.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{8.}\OperatorTok{/}\FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{        T }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.logspace(np.log10(t0), np.log10(}\FloatTok{10.} \OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{51}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        V, W }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ Jhom(T, N, alpha, model)}
\NormalTok{        ax.plot(T, V, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}+{-}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,  label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"history, $t\_0=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{t0}\SpecialCharTok{:.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{$"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        ax.plot(T, W, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}{-}{-}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,  label}\OperatorTok{=}\SpecialStringTok{f"frozen,   $t\_0=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{t0}\SpecialCharTok{:.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{$"}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{    ax.set\_xlabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{t}\CharTok{\textbackslash{},}\VerbatimStringTok{/}\CharTok{\textbackslash{},}\ErrorTok{\textbackslash{}}\VerbatimStringTok{gamma\_0}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.set\_title(}\SpecialStringTok{f"model=\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{model}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{\textquotesingle{}, $N=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{N}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{$, $}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}\textbackslash{}}\SpecialStringTok{alpha=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{alpha}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{$"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.axis([}\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{10.} \OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{3.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{15.}\NormalTok{])}
\NormalTok{    ax.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, which}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}both\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax.legend(ncol}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, loc}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}upper left\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{axes[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].set\_ylabel(}\VerbatimStringTok{r\textquotesingle{}}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{E\_0}\CharTok{\textbackslash{},}\VerbatimStringTok{J}\DecValTok{\^{}}\VerbatimStringTok{\{eff\}\_E}\KeywordTok{(}\VerbatimStringTok{t,t\_0}\KeywordTok{)}\DecValTok{$}\VerbatimStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{8_applications/ageing_creep_files/figure-pdf/cell-7-output-1.pdf}}

The results highlight three observations:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Model equivalence}: the
  \texttt{\textquotesingle{}layers\textquotesingle{}} and
  \texttt{\textquotesingle{}whole\ pores\textquotesingle{}} models yield
  identical compliance curves --- confirming that packing all shells
  into a single \texttt{sphere\_nlayers} is not an approximation but an
  exact reformulation, at a fraction of the computational cost.
\item
  \textbf{Ageing}: for early loading ages (\(t_0\) small), the creep
  compliance is much larger because many layers have not yet solidified.
  As \(t_0\) increases, the effective creep decreases toward the
  fully-set elastic limit.
\item
  \textbf{History vs frozen}: the frozen approach overestimates creep at
  early loading ages (it assumes all layers are in the state of the
  microstructure at \(t_0\), ignoring solidification history before
  that). The two approaches converge for late loading ages. The black
  dotted line shows the instantaneous elastic compliance
  \(1/E^{hom}(t)\) as a lower bound.
\end{enumerate}

\(\,\)

\chapter{API Reference}\label{sec-api}

Complete reference for the \texttt{echoes} Python module, listed in
alphabetical order. All symbols are available after
\texttt{from\ echoes\ import\ *}.

Tensor representations follow the \textbf{Kelvin-Mandel} convention:
2nd-order symmetric tensors are 6-vectors (diagonal components first,
then \(\sqrt2\) times off-diagonal); 4th-order tensors are 6×6 matrices.
See the \emph{Kelvin-Mandel notation} chapter for details.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Constants and enumerations}\label{sec-api-constants}

\subsection{Eshelby algorithm
selectors}\label{eshelby-algorithm-selectors}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Name & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{DEFAULT} & Automatic algorithm selection (recommended) \\
\texttt{RESIDUES} & Residue method (any symmetry, 3D) \\
\texttt{RESIDUES2D} & Residue method, 2D \\
\texttt{RESIDUES3D} & Residue method, explicit 3D \\
\texttt{NUMINT} & Numerical integration (anisotropic media) \\
\texttt{NUMINT2D} & Numerical integration, 2D \\
\texttt{NUMINT3D} & Numerical integration, 3D \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Homogenization scheme
selectors}\label{homogenization-scheme-selectors}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Name & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{ASC} & Asymmetric self-consistent \\
\texttt{DIFF} & Differential scheme \\
\texttt{DIL} & Dilute strain scheme \\
\texttt{DILD} & Dilute stress (compliance) scheme \\
\texttt{MAX} & Maxwell scheme \\
\texttt{MT} & Mori-Tanaka scheme \\
\texttt{PCW} & Ponte Castañeda-Willis scheme \\
\texttt{REUSS} & Reuss lower bound \\
\texttt{SC} & Self-consistent scheme \\
\texttt{VOIGT} & Voigt upper bound \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Interface type selectors}\label{interface-type-selectors}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3158}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.6842}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Name
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Description
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{NODISC} & Perfect bonding (no discontinuity) \\
\texttt{PRIMALDISC} & Primal discontinuity (displacement jump
proportional to traction) \\
\texttt{DUALDISC} & Dual discontinuity (traction jump proportional to
displacement) \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Material symmetry
selectors}\label{material-symmetry-selectors}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Name & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{ANISO} & General anisotropy (21 independent parameters) \\
\texttt{ISO} & Isotropy (2 parameters: \(k\), \(\mu\)) \\
\texttt{ORTHO} & Orthotropy (9 parameters) \\
\texttt{TI} & Transverse isotropy (5 parameters) \\
\texttt{UNDEFSYM} & Symmetry not yet determined (automatic detection) \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Viscoelastic law type
selectors}\label{viscoelastic-law-type-selectors}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Name & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{CREEP} & Creep function \(J(t,t')\) \\
\texttt{RELAXATION} & Relaxation function \(R(t,t')\) (default) \\
\end{longtable}
}

\subsection{Special 4th-order tensors}\label{special-4th-order-tensors}

All are \textbf{6×6 NumPy arrays} in Kelvin-Mandel notation (no prefix
\texttt{t}) or \textbf{\texttt{tensor\_param}} objects (prefix
\texttt{t}).

\section{Functions}\label{sec-api-functions}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{angles(obj)}}{angles(obj)}}\label{api-angles}

\emph{Rotations --- returns \texttt{list{[}float{]}}}

Extract Euler angles \((\theta,\phi,\psi)\) (in radians) from a unit
vector or a rotation matrix.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Type & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{obj} & ndarray (3,) or (3×3) & Unit vector or rotation matrix \\
\end{longtable}
}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{basis\_iso\_hill(n)}}{basis\_iso\_hill(n)}}\label{api-basis-iso-hill}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns \texttt{tuple(ndarray,\ ndarray)}}

Return the two 6×6 tensors \((\mathbf{T}_1, \mathbf{T}_2)\) spanning the
isotropic Hill polarization tensor associated with the direction
\(\uv{n}\), used in the Newton potential decomposition.

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Type & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{n} & ndarray (3,) & Unit direction vector \\
\end{longtable}
}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{change\_basis(tensor,\ R)}}{change\_basis(tensor, R)}}\label{api-change-basis}

\emph{Rotations --- returns same type as input}

Return a new tensor obtained by changing basis with rotation matrix
\texttt{R}: \(\uuuu{T}' = \mathbf{R} \star \uuuu{T}\) (full rotation).

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Type & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{tensor} & \texttt{tensor\_param} & Tensor to transform \\
\texttt{R} & ndarray (3×3) & Rotation matrix \\
\end{longtable}
}

See also: \texttt{change\_basis\_inv}, \texttt{self\_change\_basis}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{change\_basis\_inv(tensor,\ R)}}{change\_basis\_inv(tensor, R)}}\label{api-change-basis-inv}

\emph{Rotations --- returns same type as input}

Inverse basis change: \(\uuuu{T}' = \mathbf{R}^T \star \uuuu{T}\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{comp\_TI(E1,\ E3,\ nu12,\ nu31,\ mu31,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.)}}{comp\_TI(E1, E3, nu12, nu31, mu31, theta=0., phi=0.)}}\label{api-comp-ti}

\emph{Tensor builders --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Build a transversely isotropic \textbf{compliance} tensor from
engineering constants. The symmetry axis is \(\uv{e}_3\) rotated by
\((\theta,\phi)\).

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Type & Default & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{E1} & float & --- & In-plane Young's modulus \\
\texttt{E3} & float & --- & Out-of-plane Young's modulus \\
\texttt{nu12} & float & --- & In-plane Poisson's ratio \\
\texttt{nu31} & float & --- & Out-of-plane Poisson's ratio \\
\texttt{mu31} & float & --- & Out-of-plane shear modulus \\
\texttt{theta} & float & \texttt{0.} & Polar angle of symmetry axis
(rad) \\
\texttt{phi} & float & \texttt{0.} & Azimuthal angle of symmetry axis
(rad) \\
\end{longtable}
}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{crack\_compliance(shape,\ ref,\ sym=UNDEFSYM,\ algo=DEFAULT,\ ...)}}{crack\_compliance(shape, ref, sym=UNDEFSYM, algo=DEFAULT, ...)}}\label{api-crack-compliance}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Compute the \textbf{crack compliance tensor} \(\uuuu{H}\) for a flat
crack of shape \texttt{shape} embedded in reference medium \texttt{ref}:

\[\uuuu{H} = \lim_{\omega\to 0}\,\uuuu{P}(\omega,\uuuu{C}^{ref})\]

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Type & Default & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{shape} & \texttt{ellipsoidal} & --- & Crack shape (should be
flat) \\
\texttt{ref} & \texttt{tensor\_param} & --- & Reference medium
stiffness \\
\texttt{sym} & symmetry & \texttt{UNDEFSYM} & Output symmetry \\
\texttt{algo} & \texttt{ESHELBY\_ALGO} & \texttt{DEFAULT} & Numerical
algorithm \\
\texttt{epsabs} & float & \texttt{1e-8} & Absolute tolerance \\
\texttt{epsrel} & float & \texttt{1e-6} & Relative tolerance \\
\texttt{maxnb} & int & \texttt{1000} & Max integration points \\
\texttt{epsroots} & float & \texttt{1e-6} & Root-finding tolerance \\
\texttt{verbose} & bool & \texttt{False} & Print progress \\
\end{longtable}
}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{E\_from\_kmu(k,\ mu)}}{E\_from\_kmu(k, mu)}}\label{api-e-from-kmu}

\emph{Isotropic conversions --- returns \texttt{float}}

Young's modulus from bulk modulus \(k\) and shear modulus \(\mu\):
\(E = 9k\mu/(3k+\mu)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{Enu\_from\_kmu(k,\ mu)}}{Enu\_from\_kmu(k, mu)}}\label{api-enu-from-kmu}

\emph{Isotropic conversions --- returns \texttt{tuple(float,\ float)}}

Return \((E,\nu)\) from \((k,\mu)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{eshelby(shape,\ ref,\ sym=UNDEFSYM,\ algo=DEFAULT,\ ...)}}{eshelby(shape, ref, sym=UNDEFSYM, algo=DEFAULT, ...)}}\label{api-eshelby}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Compute the \textbf{Eshelby tensor} \(\uuuu{S}(\uuuu{C}^{ref},\omega)\)
for an ellipsoidal inclusion of shape \texttt{shape} in reference medium
\texttt{ref}. The Eshelby tensor relates the eigenstrain
\(\uu{\varepsilon}^*\) to the total strain inside the inclusion:
\(\uu{\varepsilon} = \uuuu{S}:\uu{\varepsilon}^*\).

Same parameter list as \texttt{crack\_compliance}.

See also: \texttt{hill}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{hill(shape,\ ref,\ sym=UNDEFSYM,\ algo=DEFAULT,\ ...)}}{hill(shape, ref, sym=UNDEFSYM, algo=DEFAULT, ...)}}\label{api-hill}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Compute the \textbf{Hill polarization tensor}
\(\uuuu{P}(\uuuu{C}^{ref},\omega) = \uuuu{S}(\uuuu{C}^{ref},\omega):\uuuu{C}^{ref,-1}\).
Same parameter list as \texttt{eshelby}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{hill\_derivative(shape,\ ref,\ index,\ sym=UNDEFSYM,\ angles={[}{]},\ algo=DEFAULT,\ ...)}}{hill\_derivative(shape, ref, index, sym=UNDEFSYM, angles={[}{]}, algo=DEFAULT, ...)}}\label{api-hill-derivative}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Derivative of the Hill tensor with respect to the \texttt{index}-th
parameter of \texttt{ref} (in the \texttt{sym} symmetry basis with
angles \texttt{angles}).

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Type & Default & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{shape} & \texttt{ellipsoidal} & --- & Inclusion shape \\
\texttt{ref} & \texttt{tensor\_param} & --- & Reference medium \\
\texttt{index} & int & --- & Parameter index (0-based) \\
\texttt{sym} & symmetry & \texttt{UNDEFSYM} & Symmetry class of
\texttt{ref} \\
\texttt{angles} & list & \texttt{{[}{]}} & Rotation angles for
\texttt{sym} \\
\ldots{} & & & Same solver options as \texttt{hill} \\
\end{longtable}
}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{hill\_dual(shape,\ ref,\ sym=UNDEFSYM,\ algo=DEFAULT,\ ...)}}{hill\_dual(shape, ref, sym=UNDEFSYM, algo=DEFAULT, ...)}}\label{api-hill-dual}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Dual Hill tensor
\(\uuuu{Q} = \uuuu{C}^{ref} - \uuuu{C}^{ref}:\uuuu{P}:\uuuu{C}^{ref}\).
Same parameters as \texttt{hill}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{hill\_taylor\_0(shape,\ ref,\ ...)}
/
\texttt{hill\_taylor\_1(shape,\ ref,\ ...)}}{hill\_taylor\_0(shape, ref, ...) / hill\_taylor\_1(shape, ref, ...)}}\label{api-hill-taylor}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Zero-th and first-order Taylor expansion of the Hill tensor around the
isotropic part of \texttt{ref}. Used for weakly anisotropic media. Same
parameter list as \texttt{hill}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{homogenize(prop,\ rve,\ scheme,\ verbose=False,\ epsrel=1e-6,\ maxnb=100,\ weight=0.,\ select\_best=False,\ concentration=False,\ fractions=\{\})}}{homogenize(prop, rve, scheme, verbose=False, epsrel=1e-6, maxnb=100, weight=0., select\_best=False, concentration=False, fractions=\{\})}}\label{api-homogenize}

\emph{Homogenization --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Compute the effective property tensor of the RVE \texttt{rve} using
scheme \texttt{scheme}.

\texttt{verbose} & bool & \texttt{False} & Print iteration details

\texttt{weight} & float & \texttt{0.} & Damping weight for SC
fixed-point iteration \\

multiple starting points \\
\texttt{concentration} & bool & \texttt{False} & Store strain
concentration tensors on each inclusion (fills \texttt{.eE},
\texttt{.eS}, \texttt{.sE}, \texttt{.sS}) \\
\texttt{fractions} & dict & \texttt{\{\}} & Override volume fractions:
\texttt{\{"PHASE":\ f,\ ...\}} \\
\end{longtable}
}

Returns a \texttt{tensor\_param} whose \texttt{.k}, \texttt{.mu},
\texttt{.E}, \texttt{.nu} give isotropic moduli.

\emph{Homogenization --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Derivative of the homogenized property tensor with respect to the
\texttt{index}-th parameter of phase \texttt{phase} (or a list of
phases).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{Id2Dn(n)} /
\texttt{tId2Dn(n)}}{Id2Dn(n) / tId2Dn(n)}}\label{api-id2dn}

\emph{Special tensors}

2D identity tensor in the plane normal to \(\uv{n}\):
\(\uu{I}_\perp = \uu{I} - \uv{n}\otimes\uv{n}\). Returns a 6×6 ndarray
(\texttt{Id2Dn}) or \texttt{tensor\_param} (\texttt{tId2Dn}).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{In(n)} /
\texttt{tIn(n)}}{In(n) / tIn(n)}}\label{api-In}

\emph{Special tensors}

4th-order tensor
\(\uuuu{I}_{\uv{n}} = \uuuu{I} - \uv{n}\uv{n}\uv{n}\uv{n}\) (identity
minus the normal dyadic projector). Returns a 6×6 ndarray (\texttt{In})
or \texttt{tensor\_param} (\texttt{tIn}).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{inv(t)}}{inv(t)}}\label{api-inv}

\emph{Tensor algebra --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Return the inverse tensor \(\uuuu{T}^{-1}\) of \texttt{t}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{invKM(v)}}{invKM(v)}}\label{api-invKM}

\emph{Tensor conversion --- returns ndarray}

Inverse Kelvin-Mandel conversion:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  6-vector → symmetric 3×3 matrix
\item
  6×6 matrix → 3×3×3×3 array (4th-order tensor)
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{isotropify(t)}}{isotropify(t)}}\label{api-isotropify}

\emph{Tensor symmetry --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Extract the \textbf{isotropic part} of tensor \texttt{t} (4th-order
ndarray or \texttt{tensor\_param}):

\[\uuuu{T}^{ISO} = \frac{\tr(\uuuu{J}:\uuuu{T})}{3}\,\uuuu{J} +
\frac{\tr(\uuuu{K}:\uuuu{T})}{5}\,\uuuu{K}\]

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{J2Dn(n)} /
\texttt{tJ2Dn(n)}}{J2Dn(n) / tJ2Dn(n)}}\label{api-j2dn}

\emph{Special tensors}

Volumetric projector in the 2D plane normal to \(\uv{n}\):
\(\uuuu{J}_\perp = \frac{1}{2}(\uu{I}-\uv{n}\uv{n})\otimes(\uu{I}-\uv{n}\uv{n})\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{K2Dn(n)} /
\texttt{tK2Dn(n)}}{K2Dn(n) / tK2Dn(n)}}\label{api-k2dn}

\emph{Special tensors}

Deviatoric projector in the 2D plane normal to \(\uv{n}\):
\(\uuuu{K}_\perp = \uuuu{I}_\perp - \uuuu{J}_\perp\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{KM(obj)}}{KM(obj)}}\label{api-KM}

\emph{Tensor conversion --- returns ndarray}

Kelvin-Mandel conversion:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  symmetric 3×3 matrix → 6-vector
\item
  3×3×3×3 array → 6×6 matrix
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{k\_from\_Enu(E,\ nu)}}{k\_from\_Enu(E, nu)}}\label{api-k-from-Enu}

\emph{Isotropic conversions --- returns \texttt{float}}

Bulk modulus from Young's modulus \(E\) and Poisson's ratio \(\nu\):
\(k = E / [3(1-2\nu)]\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{kmu\_from\_Enu(E,\ nu)}}{kmu\_from\_Enu(E, nu)}}\label{api-kmu-from-Enu}

\emph{Isotropic conversions --- returns \texttt{tuple(float,\ float)}}

Return \((k,\mu)\) from \((E,\nu)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{mu\_from\_Enu(E,\ nu)}}{mu\_from\_Enu(E, nu)}}\label{api-mu-from-Enu}

\emph{Isotropic conversions --- returns \texttt{float}}

Shear modulus from \((E,\nu)\): \(\mu = E / [2(1+\nu)]\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{newton\_potential(n)}}{newton\_potential(n)}}\label{api-newton-potential}

\emph{Eshelby --- returns \texttt{tuple(ndarray,\ ndarray)}}

Compute the Newton potential invariants
\((I_{\alpha}, I_{\alpha\beta})\) for the direction vector \texttt{n}.
Used internally for analytical Eshelby tensors.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{nn(n)} /
\texttt{tnn(n)}}{nn(n) / tnn(n)}}\label{api-nn}

\emph{Special tensors}

Symmetric dyadic product \(\uv{n}\otimes_s\uv{n}\) as a 6-vector
(\texttt{nn}) or \texttt{tensor\_param} (\texttt{tnn}).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{nu\_from\_kmu(k,\ mu)}}{nu\_from\_kmu(k, mu)}}\label{api-nu-from-kmu}

\emph{Isotropic conversions --- returns \texttt{float}}

Poisson's ratio from \((k,\mu)\): \(\nu = (3k-2\mu)/[2(3k+\mu)]\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{rot3(theta=0.,\ phi=0.,\ psi=0.)}}{rot3(theta=0., phi=0., psi=0.)}}\label{api-rot3}

\emph{Rotations --- returns ndarray (3×3)}

Build a 3×3 rotation matrix from Euler angles \((\theta,\phi,\psi)\) (in
radians). Overloaded: also accepts a list
\texttt{{[}theta,\ phi,\ psi{]}}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{rot6(theta=0.,\ phi=0.,\ psi=0.)}}{rot6(theta=0., phi=0., psi=0.)}}\label{api-rot6}

\emph{Rotations --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Kelvin-Mandel 6×6 rotation matrix corresponding to
\((\theta,\phi,\psi)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{self\_change\_basis(tensor,\ R)}
/
\texttt{self\_change\_basis\_inv(tensor,\ R)}}{self\_change\_basis(tensor, R) / self\_change\_basis\_inv(tensor, R)}}\label{api-self-change-basis}

\emph{Rotations --- in-place, returns \texttt{None}}

In-place versions of \texttt{change\_basis} /
\texttt{change\_basis\_inv}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{spheroidal(omega,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.,\ limit\_aspect\_ratio=1e-3)}}{spheroidal(omega, theta=0., phi=0., limit\_aspect\_ratio=1e-3)}}\label{api-spheroidal}

\emph{Shapes --- returns \texttt{ellipsoidal}}

Build a \textbf{spheroidal} shape descriptor with aspect ratio
\(\omega = c/a\):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\omega < 1\): oblate spheroid
\item
  \(\omega = 1\): sphere (same as \texttt{spherical})
\item
  \(\omega > 1\): prolate spheroid
\end{itemize}

The symmetry axis is \(\uv{e}_3\) rotated by \((\theta,\phi)\).
\texttt{limit\_aspect\_ratio} clips \(\omega\) to avoid degenerate
shapes.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{stiff\_Enu(E,\ nu)}}{stiff\_Enu(E, nu)}}\label{api-stiff-Enu}

\emph{Tensor builders --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Build an isotropic stiffness tensor from Young's modulus \(E\) and
Poisson's ratio \(\nu\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{stiff\_interf(k2D=0.,\ mu2D=0.,\ kn=inf,\ kt=inf,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.)}}{stiff\_interf(k2D=0., mu2D=0., kn=inf, kt=inf, theta=0., phi=0.)}}\label{api-stiff-interf}

\emph{Tensor builders --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Build a 2nd-order \textbf{interface stiffness tensor} (units:
stiffness/length).

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{stiff\_kmu(k,\ mu)}}{stiff\_kmu(k, mu)}}\label{api-stiff-kmu}

\emph{Tensor builders --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Build an isotropic stiffness tensor from bulk modulus \(k\) and shear
modulus \(\mu\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{stiff\_lambdamu(lam,\ mu)}}{stiff\_lambdamu(lam, mu)}}\label{api-stiff-lambdamu}

\emph{Tensor builders --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Build an isotropic stiffness tensor from Lamé parameters
\((\lambda, \mu)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{stiff\_TI(C1111,\ C1122,\ C1133,\ C3333,\ C2323,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.)}}{stiff\_TI(C1111, C1122, C1133, C3333, C2323, theta=0., phi=0.)}}\label{api-stiff-TI}

\emph{Tensor builders --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Build a transversely isotropic stiffness tensor from Voigt components
\((C_{1111}, C_{1122}, C_{1133}, C_{3333}, C_{2323})\). The symmetry
axis is \(\uv{e}_3\) rotated by \((\theta,\phi)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{symmetrize(t,\ sym,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.)}}{symmetrize(t, sym, theta=0., phi=0.)}}\label{api-symmetrize}

\emph{Tensor symmetry --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Project tensor \texttt{t} onto the closest material symmetry class
\texttt{sym} (axis defined by \((\theta,\phi)\)).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{tensor(array\_or\_params,\ sym=UNDEFSYM,\ angles={[}{]},\ optiangles=False,\ epsrel=1e-3)}}{tensor(array\_or\_params, sym=UNDEFSYM, angles={[}{]}, optiangles=False, epsrel=1e-3)}}\label{api-tensor}

\emph{Tensor builders --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Build a \texttt{tensor\_param} from:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a 6×6 ndarray (4th-order) or 3×3 ndarray (2nd-order),
\item
  a list or 1D array of independent parameters,
\item
  1 to 12 scalar values (for 2nd or 4th-order isotropic/TI/etc.
  representations).
\end{itemize}

\texttt{tensor\_real(t)}}{tensor\_imag(t) / tensor\_real(t)}}\label{api-tensor-real-imag}

\emph{Tensor algebra --- returns \texttt{tensor\_param}}

Extract real or imaginary part of a complex \texttt{tensor\_param}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{transverse\_isotropify(t,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.)}}{transverse\_isotropify(t, theta=0., phi=0.)}}\label{api-transverse-isotropify}

\emph{Tensor symmetry --- returns ndarray (6×6)}

Extract the \textbf{transversely isotropic part} of tensor \texttt{t}
with axis \((\theta,\phi)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{visco\_isotropify(V,\ unitsize=6)}}{visco\_isotropify(V, unitsize=6)}}\label{api-visco-isotropify}

\emph{Viscoelasticity --- returns ndarray}

Extract the isotropic part of a viscoelastic block matrix \texttt{V}
(size \(N\times N\) with \(N=\) \texttt{unitsize} × number of time
steps).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{visco\_law(function,\ visco\_law\_type=RELAXATION,\ valid\_time=0.)}}{visco\_law(function, visco\_law\_type=RELAXATION, valid\_time=0.)}}\label{api-visco-law}

\emph{Viscoelasticity --- returns \texttt{generic\_visco\_law}}

Wrap a Python callable \texttt{function(t,\ t\textquotesingle{})} into a
viscoelastic law object.

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{visco\_paramsym(V,\ sym,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.,\ psi=0.,\ unitsize=6)}}{visco\_paramsym(V, sym, theta=0., phi=0., psi=0., unitsize=6)}}\label{api-visco-paramsym}

\emph{Viscoelasticity --- returns \texttt{list}}

Extract independent parameters of viscoelastic block matrix \texttt{V}
for symmetry \texttt{sym}. Also accepts \texttt{angles} list instead of
\texttt{(theta,\ phi,\ psi)}.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{visco\_tensor(params,\ angles={[}{]})}}{visco\_tensor(params, angles={[}{]})}}\label{api-visco-tensor}

\emph{Viscoelasticity --- returns ndarray}

Reconstruct a viscoelastic block matrix from parameters \texttt{params}
and angles.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{visco\_transverse\_isotropify(V,\ theta=0.,\ phi=0.,\ unitsize=6)}}{visco\_transverse\_isotropify(V, theta=0., phi=0., unitsize=6)}}\label{api-visco-transverse-isotropify}

\emph{Viscoelasticity --- returns ndarray}

Extract the TI part of viscoelastic block matrix \texttt{V} for axis
\((\theta,\phi)\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{W(index)} / \texttt{W(index,\ n)}
/ \texttt{tW(index)} /
\texttt{tW(index,\ n)}}{W(index) / W(index, n) / tW(index) / tW(index, n)}}\label{api-W}

\emph{Special tensors}

4th-order \textbf{Walpole basis} tensor \(\uuuu{E}_{\tt index}\)
(isotropic or with normal \(\uv{n}\) for TI). Returns 6×6 ndarray
(\texttt{W}) or \texttt{tensor\_param} (\texttt{tW}). Index runs from 1
to 6 (isotropic) or 1 to 8 (TI).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{WS(index)} /
\texttt{WS(index,\ n)} / \texttt{tWS(index)} /
\texttt{tWS(index,\ n)}}{WS(index) / WS(index, n) / tWS(index) / tWS(index, n)}}\label{api-WS}

\emph{Special tensors}

\textbf{Symmetric Walpole basis} tensor. Same as \texttt{W}/\texttt{tW}
but using the symmetrised basis suited for stiffness/compliance
representations.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{Kelvin}}{Kelvin}}\label{api-Kelvin}

\emph{Scalar Kelvin chain viscoelastic law.}

\textbf{Constructor:}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Kelvin(J0, J}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[], eta}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[])}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Parameter & Type & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{J0} & float & Instantaneous compliance \\
\texttt{J} & list of float & Spring compliances \(J_i\) \\
\texttt{eta} & list of float & Dashpot viscosities \(\eta_i\) \\
\end{longtable}
}

Creep function:
\(J(t,t') = J_0 + \sum_i J_i\left(1 - e^{-(t-t')/\tau_i}\right)\),
\(\tau_i = \eta_i J_i\).

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{\texttt{Maxwell}}{Maxwell}}\label{api-Maxwell}

\emph{Scalar Maxwell chain viscoelastic law.}

\textbf{Constructor:}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Maxwell(Einf, E}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[], eta}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[])}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}

Parameter & Type & Description \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{Einf} & float & Long-term (equilibrium) stiffness \\
\texttt{E} & list of float & Spring stiffnesses \(E_i\) \\
\texttt{eta} & list of float & Dashpot viscosities \(\eta_i\) \\
\end{longtable}
}

Relaxation function:
\(R(t,t') = E_\infty + \sum_i E_i\,e^{-(t-t')/\tau_i}\),
\(\tau_i = \eta_i/E_i\).
